# 3D render: how to measure, and what every experiment concluded

**Read this before proposing or starting any render change.** It is the index and the standing rules;
it is meant to stay small. The full write-up of every experiment — landed, reverted or rejected, with
its numbers and its traps — lives in [`3d-changes.md`](3d-changes.md). Find an entry there by grepping
the heading named in the verdict table below.

Building a thing out of quads rather than generating it: [`3d-geometry.md`](3d-geometry.md) is the
how-to — the per-face texture split, the 0..1 aspect lock, values, wiring and warm.

**Appending after you try something:** write the entry in `3d-changes.md`, add **one row** here. The
row is the index; an entry with no row is invisible. Keep entries to ~25 lines — what changed, the
measured numbers, the trap, the verdict. Record settled outcomes only: no `IN PROGRESS`, no `pending`,
no "checked X and it was clean". If it is not settled, it is not an entry yet. When a new entry
invalidates an old one, mark the old heading `> SUPERSEDED by <heading>`. When `3d-changes.md` reaches
~2600 lines, `git mv` it to `3d-changes-<YYYY-MM>.md` and start a fresh one.

## Baselines

All numbers come from one of two machines, and **most of the guidance inverts between them**:

- **RTX 3050**, 60fps cap, 1080p-ish, `vidAntialias=4`. `submit`-bound: the CPU draw-call wall is the
  ceiling and a better GPU does not move it.
- **Integrated Vega** (`AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics 0x1638`), added 2026-07-28. **Fill-bound**: `GPU=`
  exceeds `submit=`, `calls` do not move with window size, and draw-call work is not the lever. See
  "the frame is GPU-bound on an integrated Radeon" in the log before applying anything here to it.

**Which view a number came from matters more than the number.** Gameplay is a zoom lerp, not a set of
modes (`RenderConfig.CAMERA`, `CameraRig`): zoom 0 = close, ~30° above ground; zoom 1 = top-down.
Resting targets are capped (`parasiteZoom: 0.30`, `hostZoom: 0.60`) and `tactical` is pinned at 1.0.
Measured across views and spots, **gameplay runs 158–288 calls**. Separately, the street-debug
**free-cam** (backtick) can go fully parallel and render the whole city — ~230 buildings — which no
gameplay camera does. It is a dev tool; the two-tier merge lead below is the only entry targeting it.

## How to measure (and how not to)

- **`calls=` in the `[street-render]` trace is the only draw-call number.** Enable the `perf street`
  console toggle (`render.Actors.DEBUG_PERF`) or street-debug mode (backtick). The trace is emitted
  by `StreetPerf.hx:228` (`frame=/GPU=/submit=/upd=/idle=/calls=/tris=/programs=/shdw=/heap=`).
- **The scene dump (`9`) is an INVENTORY, not a draw list.** `visibleDrawables` counts objects whose
  `visible` chain is true — frustum culling then discards most of them. Real ratio measured here:
  **1616 visibleDrawables → 435 calls**. A big number in the dump means nothing until you confirm
  those objects survive culling. Two failed experiments came from reading the dump as draws.
- **`submit` is the CPU draw-call wall** — time inside `composer.render()` (scene walk + issuing
  draws + any driver blocking). `upd` (engine CPU) is ~1ms and irrelevant. `GPU=` is a real timer
  query (`EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`). Compare the two before assuming which wall you are on:
  they sit at `4.75` vs `4.66` with AO off, but AO flips the frame GPU-bound. "Submit is the wall" is
  the default, not a law — and on the iGPU it is simply false.
- **Always A/B against a measured baseline in the same view.** Both `follow` and `tactical` — they
  differ (tactical draws ~80 more calls and ~2ms more submit).
- **A surface's value is `built_linear` in `art/textures.json`** — stamped for every entry by
  `make tex`, so the ladder is a grep and shows up in a diff. It is mean linear LUMINANCE (Rec.709,
  per pixel, over the OPAQUE texels), which is the statistic every row below is written in and is
  *not* the flat channel mean: on the green-dominant wilderness set the two differ by 3-9%, enough to
  invert which of two layers reads lighter. For what the manifest does not hold — a region of a
  captured frame, an atlas cell, a prop's hue split — `python3 tools/pix.py <img> [--box
  name,x0,y0,x1,y1] [--scene] [--atlas r,c] [--hue]`, same statistic. Reproduces the recorded
  asphalt 0.0329, walkway 0.0680, turf 0.0476, earth 0.0348 and roof 0.0275.

### Measuring on a weak / integrated GPU

The numbers lie unless you do all of this — every rule here was paid for by a wrong conclusion:

- **Pin the camera.** `render.Tools.freeCam` is a static, so
  `parasiteHx['render.Tools'].freeCam.lookFrom(...)` reproduces an exact frame across reloads *and*
  rebuilds (verified: 209/210/211 calls, 104,480/104,482/104,484 tris across three builds).
- **Interleave A/B/A/B and take the MEDIAN.** Means get destroyed by outliers — one run turned a real
  −19% win into a bogus −7% loss.
- **Warm the shader permutations first.** Keys `5`/`7` change the program key; the first toggle
  compiles a fresh set inside your measurement window. `(COMPILE)`-tagged trace lines are filtered.
- **Never compare across sessions.** An integrated Radeon switches power state mid-run: the identical
  pinned frame was measured at both 11.61ms and 4.06ms. Shares (`1 − off/on`) survive clock drift;
  absolute ms do not.
- **The game window must be FOCUSED and in the foreground.** Backgrounded, the compositor throttles to
  ~1fps and the timer query spans the whole idle gap, so `GPU` reads ~**1015ms** and an A/B comes out
  *inverted*. Any scripted sweep should filter samples (`GPU < 100`) and check the topbar fps.
- **Verify the thing the system is actually keyed on.** The lamp pool keys on distance to the PLAYER,
  so a free-cam-only screenshot A/B cannot see what lowering it costs — and nearly shipped a bad default.

### Attributing every draw call (the census)

`calls=` gives a total but not a culprit. To get the real per-object breakdown **without touching
shipped code**, patch from CDP (`three.global.js` exposes `window.THREE`):

- `Object3D.prototype.onBeforeRender` fires once per **post-cull** draw (`WebGLRenderer.js:2014`) —
  the only honest "what actually drew" list. Its first arg **is the renderer**, which is the one way
  to reach the live renderer from JS (`parasiteHx` is statics-only).
- Wrap `renderer.renderBufferDirect` (an **instance** property, `WebGLRenderer.js:1104`, not on the
  prototype) and read `renderer.info.render.calls` before/after each call — the delta attributes
  draws exactly, instead of assuming one call per object.
- Classify by the `scene` arg: `null` → **shadow pass** (`WebGLShadowMap` calls `renderBufferDirect`
  directly and never fires `onBeforeRender`, so shadow draws are invisible to the object hook);
  `scene.isScene === true` → main scene; anything else → **post fullscreen quad**.
- In the shadow pass `material` is the depth override — read `object.material.userData.cls` for
  identity. In the main pass `onBeforeRender` receives the **group's** material, so multi-material
  walls attribute per face (better than the dump).
- Wrap the body in `try/catch`: an exception inside the wrapper propagates into the render loop and
  silently yields an empty census.

Measured this way, **tactical = 513 calls: 175 shadow / 324 main / 14 post**.

## Verdict table

Bodies in [`3d-changes.md`](3d-changes.md) and its dated predecessors — grep the change text.

| change | result | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `flattenBox` + `mergeBand` | 10,595 → ~3,000 calls free-cam, 6 → 30fps | **landed** `6a20c75` `8240e4d` |
| MSAA via composer render targets | real AA, was previously absent entirely | **landed** `729c064` |
| Parapet ring merge per building | 583 → 436 calls, submit 10 → 7.3ms | **landed** `1183cff` |
| Ghost overlay capacity fix | fixes a hard `RangeError` crash | **landed** `5a6ae04` |
| `forceSinglePass` on decal materials | 513 → 458 calls (−11%), no visual change | **landed** |
| Merged city-wide shadow caster | shadow pass 175 → 16 calls | **landed** |
| ^ the two together, measured in-game | calls −44/−52%, **submit −25%**, +2ms idle | **landed** |
| Decals sample the atlas (per-instance UV rect) | 55 → **2** decal calls; 210 → 158 total | **landed** |
| `allowOverride:false` + `OVERLAY_EPS` | fixes 2 invisible-until-AO-on z-fight bugs | **landed** |
| GTAO ambient occlusion | +140 calls, +5.4ms GPU; flips the frame GPU-bound | **landed, default OFF** `870d0f8` |
| Roof-detail material hoist | ~750 → 6 materials; **0 draw calls** | **landed** (hygiene only) |
| Corpse-vs-blood: `depthWrite` + per-cell Y slots | killed the flip, bought per-pixel z-fight flicker | **reverted — worse** |
| Corpse-vs-blood: `renderOrder` tiers | stable layering, extra draw only in corpse cells | **landed** |
| Wall graffiti vs doors: door-span overlap skip | decals no longer clip doors | **landed** |
| Gas-burst shader pre-warm (4 programs) | first burst 167ms spike → **0 new programs** | **landed** |
| Boot pre-warm at the menu | first city entry ~2s black → **7** programs | **landed** |
| Actors + ground decals `receiveShadow` | `receiveShadow` is a UNIFORM: no recompile, no call delta | **landed** |
| Window light switches (scale-0 stand-in) | calls 151 → 151, +37 meshes, lit ratio stationary | **landed** |
| Object marks: tactical ring + through-wall x-ray | **+1 call, +2 tris** per marked object surviving cull | **landed** |
| iGPU is FILL-bound, not submit-bound | `GPU 11.2 > submit`; calls flat as the window grows | **diagnosis — inverted the ranking** |
| GPU-side A/B keys `V` / `Shift+1` / `Shift+5` | isolates render scale, bloom alone, cones alone | **landed** (permanent) |
| Render scale (`vidRenderScale`, default 100) | 1.25 → 1.00 = **−29% GPU**, zero visual change | **landed** |
| `LAMP_LIGHT.pool` 12 → 6 as a new default | −19% GPU but spends a tuned art value | **reverted** |
| ^ shipped as `vidLampLights` (Off/4/8/12, def. 12) | linear **0.32ms per light**; Off = −42% | **landed** |
| **MeshStandard → MeshLambert on city surfaces** | **−42% GPU**, calls/tris unchanged | **landed — largest win here** |
| `vidBloom` (Off/On, default On) | −3.1…3.4ms (22–24%), calls 151 → 138 | **landed** |
| AO pass was sized in CSS px | wrong AO resolution once render scale shipped | **fixed** |
| Re-attribution after Lambert | floor 32% / spots 31% / bloom 25% / shadows 15% / cones 0% | **current ranking** |
| Shadow box size (`MOON_SHADOW.halfExtent`) | best safe win ~21 calls; coverage-bound below 75 | **not a lever** |
| BatchedMesh for roof details | **+4 calls, +2ms submit, −3ms idle** | **reverted — worse** |
| Atlas the WALL textures like the decals | walls tile; an atlas cannot wrap inside a sub-rect | **rejected — wrong tool** |
| Texture array for walls (3 calls/box → 1) | ~20 calls in one sample; enables the static merge | **open lead, unmeasured** |
| Two-tier static city merge | ~3k → ~200 calls **in the debug free-cam only** | **open lead, deferred** |
| Bloom as Off / Low / High | needs the pass rebuilt; raising the threshold saves nothing | **rejected** |
| Pull the fog / view distance in | shorter sightlines — look/feel | **rejected** |
| Per-building merges of doors / covers / roof furniture | low ROI; doors are ~5 calls in view | **rejected** |
| AgX / Neutral tone mapping | look rejected | **rejected** |
| `reversedDepthBuffer` | flips custom `depthFunc`/decal offsets; doors vanish | **rejected** |
| Distance-cull far detail | parapets are the roofline silhouette — pops | **rejected** |
| SSRPass (reflections) | style rules ban glossy/specular reflections | **rejected** |
| `RenderPixelatedPass` | total aesthetic pivot, not an enhancement | **rejected** |
| `forceSinglePass` on `LightCone` as a perf item | cones measure 0% of the frame, twice | **rejected as perf** (valid hygiene) |
| Facility frame is **97.8% prop triangles** (28,424 of 1.31M is the built world) | props 3.74ms fixed + 2.6/MP, world 0.46 + 4.39/MP — props are TRIANGLE-bound, world is fill-bound | **diagnosis — inverted the ranking for prop areas** |
| Sub-pixel triangles: shrub is 4955 tris over a **42px** silhouette = 0.56px/tri | a triangle under a pixel still costs a full 2x2 quad, so the shader runs per-TRIANGLE | **standing note** |
| **glb props → `MeshLambertMaterial` (`Models.toLambert`)** | shipped build @1920x1017: gameplay **18.5 → 12.5ms**, park in frustum **29.2 → 17.5ms (30 → 47fps)** | **landed — largest facility win** |
| Park shrub stops casting (`GREEN.casts`) | −292k tris (22% of the frame) for −0.29ms — shadow tris are 0.08ms/100k | **landed** (tri count is not the cost) |

| ^ what that taught | % of frame changed is a REACH number, not a quality one — a big reach makes an unmotivated effect worse | **standing note** |
| Capturing the scene while the window is backgrounded | throttled to ~1fps, so a 500ms `onBeforeRender` capture returns nothing and reads as "mesh missing" | **trap — capture ≥2.5s** |
| ^ `SewerGround.scatter`: ledge clutter + floor decals | 8 top-down images, hash-placed per cell, merged per image → **39 → 47 calls** for a whole level | **landed** |
| ^ DARK art on a lighter surface | puddle core **1.7 vs the walkway's 43**; valve **6.7 vs the cap's 35** (0.46x in bytes = **0.19x in LINEAR**) — reads as a hole | **translucent art → `alpha`; SOLID props → new `lift` gamma in textures.json** |
| Sewer LOS (`SewerMask`): world-XZ vision mask folded into the fog | the 2D visibility polygon in cell units, baked to a canvas, sampled per material; **0 calls, 0 passes, 0 geometry**, weld intact | **landed** |
| ^ a PER-CELL mask | `sees()` takes INTEGER endpoints, so it is quantised to the player's cell and no smoothed origin can move it; no diagonal corner shadow | **replaced by the polygon** |
| ^ `userData` as the "already patched" flag | `Material.clone()` copies userData but NOT `onBeforeCompile`/`customProgramCacheKey` — every ghost clone read as patched and was not | **trap — mark the HOOK, not the material** |
| ^ patching materials by scene traverse | the actor pool / path line / tactical grid land in the same scene later, and it OVERWROTE `DecalBatch.onBeforeCompile` + its cache key | **trap — each builder patches its own; warm parity comes free** |
| ^ mask origin = the SMOOTHED slide, not `playerArea.x/y` | the logical cell snaps at action time, so the mask ran a whole `BASE_MS` **ahead** of the billboard; **9 frames per move**, idle free. In-frame A/B, 5 paired rounds, forced every frame: `upd` **+0.60**, `submit` **+0.30**, `GPU` noise, **`frame` +0.00** | **landed — no gate needed** |
| ^ canvas→GPU upload as the per-frame risk | isolated `texImage2D` reads <0.1ms at 84×56 AND 300×240 — but three's real path costs `submit` **+0.30ms** in-frame. Likely fixed overhead, not bandwidth (no scaling across 15x texels) | **small, not free — measure IN the frame, not beside it** |
| ^ the `MASK_R` cell scan | 841 cells / 3476 `canSeeThrough` **regardless of level size** (fixed window) — only the sweep grows with the level | **scan is flat; caching it buys 0.1ms, skipped** |
| ^ actors still gate on `sees()` at the logical cell | mask and actors used to snap TOGETHER; smoothing makes actors **lead** the mask by up to one move (opposite of the accepted corner divergence) | **accepted — `FADE_SPEED` reads it as a lead** |
| ^ **corner-only rays leave one cell unreachable** | the step past a hit reveals the cell LEFT of a left corner and RIGHT of a right one, so the cell the origin straddles (`floor(ox)`/`floor(oy)`) is aimed at by nothing and stayed dark — measured col 10 **0.00** vs 9/11 at **1.00**; only shows on a STRAIGHT run | **4 cardinal rays (of ~270) — complete, not a patch** |
| ^ the mask edge was hard only where it was **axis-aligned** | canvas AA writes no partial coverage on a texel-aligned straight edge: scanline through the player's row was a clean step, diagonals ramped 2-3 texels (107 mid of 4704) | **`MASK_PX` 4→2 — WRONG LEVER, reverted; it was the per-cell wall reveal** |
| ^ `MASK_WALL_FADE`: a lit wall fades toward masonry the sweep never reached | per TEXEL, because a cell can fade two ways at once and the value is the MIN — no gradient stack gives that; only runs at the ENDS of a run. Measured `255,191,63` ramps and **0/294 cells bleed red into an unreached wall** | **landed — floor keeps the exact polygon** |
| ^ wallness in the mask's **GREEN** channel gates the wobble | static per level → painted once, blitted in as the clear; everything after composites `'lighter'` and writes RED ONLY so visibility cannot trample it. **294/294 cells match masonry** | **landed — 2 fetches of a tiny cached texture** |
| ^ ground debris drew at FULL brightness where the player cannot see | it rides `DecalBatch`, which owns an `onBeforeCompile` — so `patch()` now **chains** a pre-existing hook instead of replacing it (different anchors, both survive) | **landed — `decalInstanceAlphasewerMaskb`** |
| ^ **chaining `customProgramCacheKey` naively BLANKS THE FRAME** | three's `Material` has a DEFAULT key on the PROTOTYPE returning `this.onBeforeCompile.toString()` — never null, and unbound `this` throws on every draw: 838 errors, **0 draw calls** | **`hasOwnProperty` + `Reflect.callMethod(mat, …)`** |
| ^ where to patch a lazily-built, actor-owned material | `DecalBatch` groups are built on first paint, so no build-time moment — `View`'s loop patches after `actors.update`, gated on `SewerArea` (uniforms are static, `Actors` is per-area). Expose MATERIALS not meshes: `grow()` swaps the mesh, keeps the material | **landed** |
| ^ `ensure()` early-outs on matching dimensions | every habitat is 21×14, so anything cached per-level on SIZE alone is the previous level's — `buildWallLayer` runs from `attach` unconditionally | **trap — size is not level identity** |
| ^ world-space sine wobble on the sample UV | `MASK_WOBBLE` 1.2 world units, two octaves, keyed on `vSewerMask` ONLY — world-keyed so the polygon slides THROUGH it; player-keyed would make the whole boundary swim every move | **landed — masonry only, no new programs** |
| ^ a canvas blur to widen the ramp | benchmark read 1.6ms at 84×56 and **0.5ms at 300×240** — cheaper at 15x the pixels, so it measured the `getImageData` flush, not the blur | **rejected then — SUPERSEDED, see `MASK_BLUR`** |
| ^ **the floor boundary was ONE antialiased texel** | scanline `0 0 0 0 [96] 255 255`, a clean 45°; **55 intermediate texels over the whole floor boundary**. A texel is 1 world unit = ~30-38 screen px, so bilinear of that single coverage value staircases at exactly that period | **the sweep was innocent — raster, not geometry** |
| ^ `MASK_BLUR` 0.75 texels, on a SCRATCH canvas | red visibility plane painted separately and composited through `ctx.filter` over an unblurred `wallLayer` — blurring the finished mask would smear the GREEN channel the wobble gates on. After: `0 1 8 41 116 199 243 254 255`, intermediates **55 → 311**, `greenNotPure` **0** | **landed** |
| ^ re-measuring the blur with the flush AMORTIZED | 200 composites per sample, A/B/A/B ×5, median: **+0.0765ms at 84×56, +0.0815ms at 300×240** — flat across 15x the pixels, so fixed per-call overhead, not fill. σ 2.0 costs `+0.0985 / +0.1035` — flat in RADIUS too | **cheap — the 1.6ms was the flush; σ is free, the filter CALL costs** |
| ^ `MASK_BLUR` 0.75 → **2.0**, and what a wider σ buys | band/dim/bleed swept live: `0.75→867/1.5%/1.9`, `1.5→1485/5.6%/5.2`, `2.0→1826/14.6%/7.5`, `2.5→2149/18.8%/9.8`, `4→2874/39.1%/17.4`. A CONVOLUTION — the band only widens by eating the lit side or spilling onto the hidden one | **2.0 is the knee; past ~2.5 a 1-cell corridor stops reaching full brightness** |
| ^ **a bigger σ FIGHTS the border fade** | `fadeCell` authors its ramp inside ONE cell (4 texels) and a σ2 kernel spans ~8, so it averages back up: border cap outermost texel **79 (vis 0.434) → 103 (vis 0.511)**. And mask 0 still renders at `MASK_HIDDEN` 0.18 | **the level's outer silhouette is NOT a blur problem — wants lower `MASK_HIDDEN` or a post-blur `rgb(v,255,255)` multiply layer** |

Measured world radii vs the authored standoffs: pile-1 **1.425** vs 0.55, cable **0.830** vs 0.50, bags **0.825** vs 0.40 (drum and crates were fine by luck) | **fixed — `margin` replaced by `r`, the footprint radius per unit of height measured off the glb; `SewerProps` derives `margin = r * h + PROP_CLEAR`. all five now clear their wall by 0.05, verified headless** |

A crate at h 0.75 / standoff 0.35 crosses the wall plane at y **1.22** against a 3.0 cap — not partly occluded, gone at every zoom. Clearing it would need a standoff of 1.7, i.e. mid-corridor | **fixed — `SewerProps.CAM_DIR` drops dir 1 from `faces`; `PROP_PCT` 14 → 18 pays back the ~quarter of faces lost. proof: max \|yaw\| **1.923** < the π/2+jitter limit 2.071, where a south face would be ≥ 2.642** |

Drum and crates are both `corner: true` and share those spots (crates also went `h` 1.1 → 1.65) | **landed — verified against the floor grid rather than the placement code: drum and crates 1/1 each on real corners, the three flat props 0/12, none off a floor cell** |

Not the seed either: a fresh seed came back *brighter* and bimodal pink/black, strictly worse | **fixed at the REFERENCE — repainted mid-charcoal (subject luma p05 30 → 48, the band the three correct props sit in) and the same generator returned 67/70/78, cool and neutral, tracking the reference hue. `baseColor` is now only a uniform 0.47 darken. | ^ so each pool is DEALT as a deck, not rolled | per-pool deck, Fisher-Yates'd off the same hash chain on refill (level stays determined by its saved grid), popped one spot at a time. demo tunnel flat sequence is a full permutation per cycle, **max run 1**; live row 8 went `drum,drum,drum,drum,crates` → `drum,drum,crates,crates,drum` | **landed — max run 4 → 2, corner 4/2 → 3/3, flat cable-6-of-7 → 2/2/1/1/1, placement invariants unchanged** |

| A failed glb load stalled BOOT, not just the prop | `Models.get`'s error path dropped its waiters and `View.warmup` sequences on one — two 404s left the warm Promise unresolved, so `comp.dispose()` and `setRenderTarget(null)` never ran and the renderer stayed bound to the warm target | **fixed — waiters get an empty template (which must carry a child Group: `instanced` reads `pivot.children[0]` blind)** |
| **The tunnels read as boxes — and it is the CAP EDGE, not the masonry** | wall faces are 0.67% of the view vs the cap tops' 14.68%, and `wall.png` is already broken brick + moss. | ^ `CAP_CHAMFER` 0.25: a 45° wedge between face and cap | face stops at `WALL_H - c`, a second quad carries it up and back; the cap insets by the same `c` on every edge overlooking floor. Exactly paired (a cap edge insets *iff* its neighbour walls it), so the plateau never opens. Swept live: **0.15 invisible, 0.45 a chunky moulding, 0.25 right** | **landed — this is the rejected ledge-rim darkening done as geometry, which is why it works** |
| ^ **a bevel on a plan OUTLINE must be MITRED — shipped without it and quads hung in the air** | run a wedge along a whole cell edge and at an OUTSIDE corner its last `c` is over open corridor (both walls have receded by `d` at height `fh+d`) — two wedges crossing in mid-air past the corner. Dual bug at an INSIDE corner: a `c × c` notch straight through to the background | **fixed — classify each END off `diag`/`perp`** |
| ^ the three-case rule, and why the inside case is NOT "extend the top edge" | `diag` floor → pull IN by `c` (**no extra triangle**, the quad becomes a trapezoid; the two wedges already share their bottom corner, so closing the top closes the seam). `diag` solid + `perp` floor → straight run, nothing. `diag` solid + `perp` solid → a vertical return triangle, i.e. a real chamfer STOP. Extending instead slides the wedge under the diagonal cell's un-inset cap and opens a void — that cap would then need an L notch, 4 tris instead of 2 on the biggest surface down there | **landed — +1 tri per inside end, 0 per outside end** |
| ^ verified headless, and the OBVIOUS test is the wrong one | demo: 84 faces / 8 outside ends / 16 inside ends → **352 wall tris vs 84×4+16 = 352 exactly**, and **all 136 interior wedge-top verts land on a corner of the inset cap, 0 misses**. "Is the vertex over a solid cell?" passes on the BROKEN build — the overshoot is inside the cell's *footprint*, just above its bevelled surface | **trap — test the seam, not the footprint** |
| ^ `vertexColors` on the shell, keyed off the cell LATTICE | ±`TINT_AMP 0.15` hashed per lattice point (rounded cell index + height band), read at each quad corner. `MeshBuf` gives every quad its own verts, so per-face would step at every boundary — the two-wall-variant seam. Same channel carries `TINT_FOOT 0.80` / `TINT_CORNER 0.75` / `TINT_CHAMFER 1.08` | **landed — free unevenness, no art; a lattice key cannot seam by construction** |
| ^ `FLOOR_TILE`/`LEDGE_TILE` 8.0 → **7.0** | both were exactly 2 cells, so the texture period landed on the same point of the tile at every grid line — a visible pattern on the two surfaces that fill the screen. 7.0 pushes the echo to 7 cells | **landed — one constant** |
| ^ its cost | **not measured.** +1 quad per wall face (104 in the habitat) is the honest inventory number; window would not foreground, HUD held **1 FPS**, topbar swung 50–56 dc / 69.9–70.6k tri on pose alone | **open — same limitation as the props entry above** |
| ^ south (`dir 1`) wall faces are backface-culled in EVERY pose | normal `(0,0,-1)` against a camera always at +Z looking −Z, so their grime and decals (~a quarter of both passes) are pure waste. The wall quads are NOT free to drop: `casts = true`, and the shadow map renders from the light | **found, not acted on** |
| Contact shadows stood UP an inside wall corner | **nothing in the tunnel lighting can darken one**: ambient is normal-blind, hemi keys on `normal.y` and both faces of a vertical corner are vertical, and a corridor spot sees both walls. GTAO is the only engine answer at +140 calls, off by default. Rides the floor strips' own `InstancedMesh` — same quad, same material, same gradient — so habitat **21 inside corners → 32 strips, batch 102 → 134, +0 draw calls, +0 programs, 0 art** | **landed** |
| ^ a yaw alone cannot place one | the gradient runs along the quad's local +Y, so standing it up needs a quarter turn about local Z first — and WHICH one depends on which END of the face the corner is, since yaw sets normal and fade direction together. A mirror would flip the winding off `FrontSide` | **two base quaternions, picked by `-nz*ax + nx*az < 0`** |
| ^ and they must MEET, not cross | each strip stands `EPS` off its own wall, so one running from the bare corner pokes that far past its perpendicular twin — a small double-dark cross. Each starts `EPS` along the wall too, which is exactly the twin's stand-off | **same lesson as the chamfer mitre** |
| ^ draw order was a real bug | all of it is transparent + `depthWrite:false`, and every piece is a level-wide merge with origin `(0,0,0)` — so three compares an identical sort `z` and falls through to `a.id - b.id`, i.e. **construction order**. `grime, shadows, decals` let an `alphaTest`'d crack decal REPLACE the corner shadow | **fixed — `grime, decals, shadows`; `WALL_SHADOW_EPS 0.07` also clears `DECAL_EPS 0.05`** |
| ^ `TINT_CORNER` 0.75 → **0.90** | the vertex term and the strip multiply: 0.75 under a 0.55-alpha black put the corner at **0.34 of base**, a hole. The vertex term is now the broad wash under the strip, and the only cover for the chamfer/stop above its `WALL_H - CAP_CHAMFER` top | **landed** |
| ^ verified headless before looking | demo: **96 = 84 flat + 12 vertical** against 8 inside corners with 4 camera-side (8×2−4). Every vertical instance decomposed: spans y 0→2.75, exactly `WALL_SHADOW_W` wide, normal into a floor cell with masonry behind, opaque end at exactly 0.07 both off the wall AND along it from the corner lattice. **0 failures on 5 checks** | **the decomposition is the check** |
| **The cap stops being LEVEL — `CAP_SAG 0.4`, hashed per LATTICE point** | the chamfer split the silhouette into two edges but left both dead level, which is the other half of "reads as a box". `capY(x,z)` hashes the *rounded* cell index like `tint` does, so height is a property of a grid POINT: two cells share their corner heights, a wall tilts between its run's two, and the cap's `CAP_CHAMFER` inset rounds back to the point it came from. Downward only — `WALL_H` is the camera-clearance number | **landed — 5.7° tilt per cell, 0 extra tris, 0 extra calls, 0 art** |
| ^ why the report's per-CELL version was the expensive one | a per-cell height STEPS, and a step between two solid cells is a hole through the plateau → a filler quad on every solid/solid boundary. That filler then meets the chamfer: full-width it stands a zero-thickness fin `c` tall through the bevel, inset to the cap outline it leaves a `c`-wide notch. Three new cases, i.e. the mitre problem again on new geometry | **trap — a lattice key has no steps, so it has no fillers and no cases** |
| ^ what it costs: nothing may assume `WALL_H` | decals clamped to `WALL_H - CAP_CHAMFER` → `faceH`, the LOWER of the face's two corners. Corner shadow strips the same, interpolated over `WALL_SHADOW_W` — they stand OFF the wall, so overshooting a descending edge shows a black sliver with nothing to hide it. Ledge clutter sat at `WALL_H + LEDGE_DECAL_Y` → `capAt` per decal corner, evaluated on the same two triangles `cap()` emits | **3 consumers, all real; the floor pass is genuinely flat and keeps its constant** |
| ^ verified headless | **every cap vertex on `capY` of its own lattice point, 0 off**, none above `WALL_H`, none below `WALL_H − CAP_SAG` (range 2.601–3.000). Wall tris **352 = 84×4 + 16 exactly**, so the sag added no geometry, and the mitre seam test still shows **0 interior misses**. Ledge decals 284 verts all at exactly `LEDGE_DECAL_Y` over the surface, max tilt 0.182 within one decal. Vertical strips 12, tops 2.370–2.664 (were all 2.750), **0 over their own wall** | **landed** |
| **The sag shipped a hairline down every inside corner** | a 1px pure-black line out of every concave corner, texture and shading continuous across it — both sides are cap. `cap()` sampled `capY` at corners the chamfer had pulled back, and `capY` rounds an inset corner to the lattice point it came from. The un-inset neighbour draws the same two lattice heights over the FULL cell, the inset one over 3.75 → `gap = (CAP_CHAMFER/CELL)·Δh`, max **0.025** | **fixed — off-lattice vertices take `capAt`, not `capY`** |
| ^ why inside corners only | a cap edge-end insets iff its perpendicular neighbour is floor, and two cells across a boundary disagree about that exactly where the corridor turns concave. A straight run agrees at both ends and is watertight | **the rest of the plateau was never affected** |
| ^ the trap: it is TWO samplers, not one | the wall FACE top must stay on the lattice (`capY - k`) — give it `capAt` and the two perpendicular faces of an inside corner sample at their own inset offsets and the crack moves into the wall. Chamfer stop likewise: apex on the lattice (`capY`), other top vertex on the inset plane (`capAt`). Face and wedge stop being exactly `k` apart, by ≤0.025 | **landed** |
| ^ the demo could not see it — scan a random grid | boundary-edge scan (weld, count edge usage, keep count-1, find a vertex in the plan-interior of one at a different height) on 44×44 at 50% floor: **295 cracks, max 0.0247, mean 0.0082, all on the ledge mesh, all on a cell boundary → 0.** Demo still 352 wall tris, 0 interior seam misses | **a regular tunnel is not a test case** |
| ^ corner shadow strip put its error in the WRONG place | one instance matrix cannot taper a quad, so a tilting bevel means the rectangle disagrees somewhere. `min` of its two ends puts a lit sliver (≤0.072) **in the corner**, where the gradient is fully opaque | **fixed — take the corner's own height; the far end, where the gradient is 0, is the end that disagrees** |
| **The habitat's four objects become glb props** | biomineral / assimilation / preservator / watcher off the atlas and through `ObjModels`. A/B in one pose, twice: **56 → 60 calls, 77.7k → 97.7k tris**; boot 73 → **76** programs | **landed** |

| ^ ghost/hull batches cost 0, and the warm covers them | `prog` held at **87** across clear/build/tactical (no first-use compile); standing on a prop is **+1**. The predicted +12 boot programs was wrong — all five props share one material permutation | **measured** |
| ^ split ratio picked the workflow per prop, before spending | preservator **1.34x** (drum's class → offline decimate, `error` 0.01 = 48 texels/tri, 98,685 → 5,452); biomineral **2.41x** / assimilation **2.24x** / watcher **2.46x** → regenerated at `decimation_target 5000`, `tris: -1`. All four MR maps pure green, no `dropMR` | **the rule held again** |
| ^ the assimilation reference was repainted BEFORE generating | first restyle sat at subject luma p05 **34** — between bags' failing 30 and its fixed 48 — and already R>G, B≫G, the violet signature. Repainted to p05 **61**. Others passed first time (56/43/44 vs `drum`'s 41) | **caught by measuring, not by looking** |
| ^ emissive DERIVED from the baked albedo, not hand-painted | a TRELLIS atlas is not paintable freehand, so each glow is keyed out of the prop's own base map (same UVs by construction). Bands from measured percentiles — **guessing gave 0/0/1/100% lit**. Preservator needs an **R-B** key (veins −14, amber core +69); luma cannot separate it | **landed — no `models.mjs` change** |
| ^ `hab/habitat [all\|<name>\|clear] [level]` console command | the normal path needs a live host and kills it, so all four cost a spawn-attach-harden-invade cycle each to look at | **landed — `console/HabitatConsole.hx`** |
| **One zero-length vertex normal blacks out the WHOLE frame** | reported as flicker-on-mouse-move going black. Composite measured mean luma **0.24/255** at a healthy 60 FPS / 67 calls. `normalize(vec3(0.0))` is NaN, one NaN texel goes through UnrealBloom's 1/32 downsample + gaussian and poisons every pixel. Bisection: mask innocent (floor→1.0 lifts 0.24→0.53), lights all on, **direct-to-canvas render 14.28**, **bloom off 14.15** | **fixed — `fixNormals()` in `tools/models.mjs`, area-weighted face normals, run AFTER `simplify()`** |
| ^ never habitat-only — it shipped in the sewers | `sewer/bags` **68** zero normals, `biomineral` 17, `cable` 2, `crates` 1, `assimilation` 1, rest 0. Biomineral is always on screen, a bags pile usually is not — that is the whole difference. Intermittency IS the mechanism: neighbours interpolate non-zero, so only a pixel landing near that one vertex goes NaN | **0 zero normals in all 14 built glbs; 0 NaN over 12 sweeping frames; 10 frames at mean 14.24-14.99** |
| ^ `attributes.normal.array` is a LIE on a GLTFLoader glb | interleaved buffers → every attribute reports the SAME length (60,720 for a 7,590-vertex prop) and a stride-3 walk reads positions as normals. Invented zeros that were not there and hid the real ones | **trap — use `attr.getX/getY/getZ(i)` + `attr.count`, never `.array`** |
| ^ a bake-STEP change does not rebuild anything | `last_sig` only covered per-entry params, so a fixed pipeline would have left every existing glb broken | **new `PIPELINE` constant folded into the sig — bumping it rebuilt all 14** |
| **The organs' glow moves off the surface and into the air** | emissive OFF on all four, coloured `PointLight` pool ON (`particles/PropLights.hx`, `PROP_LIGHT`). The two were never a pair — an emissive map brightens the prop's own texels and lights NOTHING around it. Colour per row in `ObjModels.MODELS.light`, each organ's own former glow hue. `prog` **87 → 85**, glbs −8% | **landed — art values open** |
| ^ built in `SewerScene.build`, NOT `SewerArea.build` | `View.warmup` builds its warm tunnel scene by calling that function, so the pool is present when `compileAsync` walks it and `NUM_POINT_LIGHTS` (5 → **9**) matches warm and real. A level later instead, every lit tunnel material recompiles on first habitat entry | **verified — `prog` went DOWN, not up** |
| ^ `emissiveStrength: 0` now skips the map entirely | baking a map nobody sees still costs a dead texture in the glb, a `USE_EMISSIVEMAP` permutation and a fetch per fragment. `emissiveSrc` stays in the manifest, so re-enabling one prop is a single number | **landed — 2 fewer programs** |

| ^ `h` **4.06 → 3.6**: height is a poor proxy for how much CELL a prop eats | the bbox is 1.36 h wide and 1.29 h *deep*, nearly all of it the flat root pool (47% of the surface area), so 4.06 would have laid a 5.5-unit pool over a 4-unit cell | **3.6 → ~4.9, the width the assimilation arch already overhangs to** |
| ^ the glow keys on **`g / b`**, and blue alone is the discriminator | pod and cage carry nearly the same red and green and differ **6.7× in blue**. Measured per triangle, area-weighted, linear: cage **0.5**, pool brown **1.3**, amber **2.5-3.8** → `[1.9, 2.7]`. `min(r,g)/b` is byte-identical on this atlas (g never exceeds r), so that fallback is dead | **new `PropKey` enum on `PropShine`; the value rides `customProgramCacheKey`, as the mote count already does** |
| ^ **`moteR` 0.20 was too big, and "too big" is not what it looked like** | at that reach ONE mote covers the whole amber face, so three add to a flat uniform gold panel and the pod's own painted shading vanishes under it — a lit surface, not something lit from inside. 0.13 (4× the biomineral's radius, ~15× its area) is a discrete blob rising inside a shell | **an effect must be SMALLER than the region it travels, or there is nothing to see it travel against** |
| ^ the mask band is deliberately **soft** here (~20% ramping vs the biomineral's 4%) | a hard crystal-against-flesh edge was right there and is wrong here: a soft mask IS the gradient of light leaking through a shell. Only affordable because `mote` is 0.08 — at the biomineral's amplitude a ramp this wide would wash half the prop | **landed — and `yLo` starts the climb above the root pool, which a 3-mote budget cannot afford to spend on a masked-out base** |
| ^ `glow: 1.0` is not a placeholder | every other glowing thing here sets it past 1 to clear `BLOOM_THRESHOLD` 0.9; this is the one that must NOT. Level lives in `base` 0.055 (~52 sRGB over every amber texel) and `mote` 0.38 — peak total 0.435 = linear luminance **0.285**, where the biomineral's motes are ~20× their surface and clip | **no halo, by construction** |
| ^ **`base` is the dominant term on this prop, `mote` is not** | the mask covers the whole pod while three motes cover a fifth of it, so "brighter overall" is `base`. `mote` must be raised WITH it or the travelling blob stops reading against the floor it crosses. The cost of a high `base` is relative contrast — an additive constant over an albedo whose own variation is smaller than it flattens the painted internal shading as it rises | **that is the dial to turn if the amber ever looks like a panel again** |
| ^ its cost, A/B on one pinned free-cam pose, same protocol both legs | `calls` **57 / 57** — zero draw-call delta. `progs` **99 on / 91 off** = the 8 prop permutations, all present after boot warm, none compiling on habitat entry. Keys landed as designed: `propAnimSUL` solid+ghost, `propAnimSUP` on the unlit tactical hull, `propGlow3AMBER`; biomineral untouched. 0 console errors, 60 FPS | **free at this resolution** |
| ^ motion verified against the GEOMETRY alone | emissive killed with debug key `6` so the glow could not be mistaken for it; two frames 5.2s apart (half a ~11s breath) put the top diff-energy tile squarely on the pod, apex rising ~6% of the prop's height | **the pulse is live and unmasked by the glow** |
| **The preservator shipped ALMOST BLACK: its regenerated MR map came back metallic** | "almost completely dark, with a little bit of specular" is the exact metalness signature, not a vague complaint — a metal has **no diffuse term**, its base colour becomes F0, and underground there is no env map, so nothing is left but the highlight | **fixed — `"dropMR": true`, `sewer-pile-2`'s case exactly** |
| ^ read the MR map; glTF packs **G = roughness, B = metalness** | preservator blue mean **211** (p05 190, p95 229) = ~0.83 metal over the whole prop, against assimilation 44.7, watcher 3.5, biomineral 0. `metallicFactor` was 1 and glTF MULTIPLIES it by the map. The 2048² **source** carries it too, so it came out of TRELLIS, not the bake. Base colour was never the problem: mean sRGB 83/50/45, in family | **one dump answers it outright — do not reason about it** |
| ^ confirmed live with debug key **`M`** BEFORE changing anything | under force-matte the same pods render exactly as the reference art. That is what separates "the material is metallic" from "metallic AND that is the whole story" — and it cleared the pulse and glow shaders, which were the obvious suspects for having just landed on this prop | **always A/B the material before suspecting your own code** |
| ^ **the trap is documentation, not code** | the retired mesh's entry said "MR map pure green, no dropMR" and that line was carried into the rewritten note for the regenerated one without re-measuring. TRELLIS is not bit-deterministic and metalness is a per-generation lottery (`sewer-pile-1` green, `sewer-pile-2` cyan, same generator, same day) | **re-read the MR map after every regeneration: a past verdict is a claim about a FILE, not about a subject** |
| ^ dropping the map also discards its roughness VARIATION, and here that is a feature | this prop's green channel spans p05 132 → p95 233, so keeping it would put 5% of the surface at effective roughness 0.18 — glossier than any other organ. `roughness: 0.35` re-applies after `dropMR` and lands where the family already sits (biomineral 0.345, watcher 0.25, arch 0.45). glb **1123KB → 747KB** | **landed** |
| ^ knock-on: the glow's `base`/`mote`/`moteR` all had to go **up** | an emissive term is judged as a RATIO to the lit surface under it, and a black surface flatters any addition — fixing the material moved the target by about what the material had been swallowing | **tune emissive LAST, and never on a prop whose material is still in question** |
| **Every habitat organ now takes `PropYaw.FRONTAL`, not just the arch** | each was generated by TRELLIS from a single front-on reference, so each has exactly one authored face and the rest of the body is the back of that drawing — the arch's case was never special. Measured as area-weighted albedo luminance per 30° of azimuth: **all four peak in or immediately beside the camera-facing bin**, so unturned really is each prop's best side (arch 90-120°, preservator 90-120°, watcher 60-90° straddling it, biomineral 120-150°) | **landed — `yawFix` corrects only street-lamp2, so FRONTAL shows whatever the glb was authored with; measure before trusting it** |
| ^ the watcher had the most to lose | its pale eye discs ARE its read — the reason that row carries `baseColor 0.4` instead of a straight fit to the family mean — and a hashed roll that put them round the back left a blank lump | **landed** |
| ^ the cost is real and accepted: several of a kind in a room are now visible **clones** | what breaks them up is not the pose but the MOTION — sway, swell, twist, motes and strikes all take a phase hashed off the instance's own **translation**, so they never move in step | **`HASHED` kept for clutter, which has no front at all** |
| ^ stale RATIONALE is its own bug class | `PropGlow`'s per-instance camera azimuth was justified by "yaw is HASHED per placement"; that reason is now false while the code is still right — the real reason is the per-instance **translation**, since two props in different cells see the camera in different directions | **fixed the comment, not the code — the MR-map entry above is what a carried-forward stale claim costs** |
| **The preservator's glow was invisible because it never CROSSED the bloom threshold** | shipped at `glow 1.0`: peak `base 0.055 + mote 0.38` = 0.435 against a tint of linear luminance 0.654 = **0.285 vs BLOOM_THRESHOLD 0.9**. It could not halo by construction, which is what "faint and subtle" had been implemented as | **a brightness that never crosses the threshold is a SHADE, not a glow** |
| ^ the fix is a term that STRADDLES it, not a bigger one | new `pulse`/`pulseRate` on `PropShine` scaling the whole `glowSum` by `1 - pulse + pulse*(0.5+0.5*sin(...))`, phased per instance off the mote hash. At `glow 3.0 / base 0.035 / mote 0.85 / pulse 0.6`: mote peak **1.74** at the breath's top and **0.70** at its bottom, resting shell 0.048-0.069 and never blooming | **landed — the halo appears and fades on a ~14s cycle** |
| ^ `base` went **down** while the pod got **brighter** | `glow x base` is what matters: 0.055 → **0.105**, a ~1.9x brighter shell off a smaller `base`. Read every `shine` amplitude as a product with `glow`, never on its own | **the trap that makes these rows look self-contradictory** |
| ^ both new fields are **uniforms, not literals** | so `customProgramCacheKey` is untouched — verified live, still `propGlow3AMBER`, `__progs()` holds at **100**. A row retunes or disables the breath with nothing to compile and nothing to add to the boot warm | **put a knob in a uniform unless GLSL forces a literal (as the mote COUNT does)** |
| **The preservator TETHERS its preserved hosts — a bowed cord on the lightning's own ribbon** | new `tether` column + `PropTether`; `drawTethers` appends into the SAME buffers `boltRibbon` fills, with the station emitter factored to `ribbonStation`/`ribbonQuads`. `bolt*` fields renamed `ribbon*` | **landed — measured `calls 72`, `submit 2.6ms`, `GPU 4.59ms`, 60 FPS, `prog 100`** |
| ^ the cost is **+1 draw call worst case**, not zero and not one per cord | the mesh existed but sat `visible=false` whenever no bolt was alight (`duty 0.18`, dark ~30% of the time); a live cord keeps it up. Sharing one mesh is why four cords are still that same one call | **say the bound, do not round it to zero** |
| ^ a cord is not a bolt: bell it at **both** ends and keep brightness **flat** | bow and waver are belled to 0 at each end (a cord is attached twice; a bolt only has to start on the body), and there is no along-span falloff — a link that dims toward the far end stops reading as a link | **landed** |
| ^ `haloDim 0.65` made it a glowing TUBE | `0.65 x 2.2 x 0.654 = 0.94`, over threshold, so the 0.3-world flare bloomed in its own right. At **0.5** (0.72, under) the flare is a soft skirt and only the ~4px core straddles — 1.44 peak / 0.50 trough | **on a thin ribbon the flare buys bloom AREA; let it self-bloom and it stops being a flare** |

| ^ `tilt` is a signed MAGNITUDE now, not a spread | `f.tilt * (0.6 + 0.8*h3) * (h5 < 0.5 ? -1 : 1)` on a fifth roll for the sign. At `tilt 0.45` every ring leans 15-36° and two still lean opposite ways, so a swarm's circles are not parallel to each other either. `0` is the only way back to flat | **landed** |
| ^ the height variation is the LEAN, not the roll | row moved to `y 0.80 / yVar 0.10` — centre in the upper third, kept tight so it cannot leave it — and the tilt then swings the dot `r*sin(tilt)` = 0.33 h either side, lapping ~0.47 h up over the crown. Measured half an orbit apart (~2.6s at `rate 0.18`): **~75 px up the screen** and across to the far side | **a ring that merely sits high is still a hoop** |
| **The ribbon layer split out of `PropFX` into `render.actors.Ribbon`** | not a line-count refactor: `PropFX` was 780 lines but **34% comment** (~513 code), and 5th largest in `render/`. | ^ the reason that mattered: a **NaN guard living in two hand-copies** | `drawArcs` and `drawTethers` carried character-identical width-axis blocks (cross product, length, zero-length guard, normalize) differing only in variable names. Its failure mode is not cosmetic — one NaN texel through the bloom downsample blacks out the whole frame. Now `Ribbon.widthAxis`, once | **a guard surviving on copy discipline is a matter of time** |
| ^ and the one-mesh invariant became STRUCTURAL | sharing three arrays is the entire reason a level's every bolt and cord is ONE call. As a convention between two functions it was a thing to remember; as a class it cannot be got wrong unnoticed | **landed** |
| ^ `BoltOpts` / `TetherOpts` agreed on **14 of ~15 fields**, comments and all | both now extend a shared `RibbonPass`, adding only `arc`/`bucket`/`slot` and `tether` respectively | **landed — 780 -> 627 in PropFX, +230 Ribbon** |
| ^ what deliberately did NOT split: core and flies | 61 code lines between them, and the four effects share a clock referenced BY IDENTITY by the core material, one per-frame object walk, and one hash. | ^ the preservator's rule was too narrow | it says re-read the MR MAP after every regeneration. It should say re-read every MEASURED constant — here the MR map (0/214/5, rough dielectric) was the one thing that had NOT changed | **standing note** |
| ^ hand-parsing a **BAKED** glb's vertices returns plausible garbage | gltf-transform writes INTERLEAVED (`byteStride: 32` = POSITION 12 + UV 8 + NORMAL 12); the TRELLIS source does not. A flat `Float32Array` read gave eye clusters OUTSIDE the bounding box and 76 single-tri clusters from 79 hits, misdiagnosed as `tex: 512` gutter bleed | **trap — measure the SOURCE (with `tris: -1` the geometry is identical), or honour `byteStride`; assert against the accessor's own min/max** |

| ^ the failed gate is the useful half: 3D distance from the body's centre | **797 of the 980 vertices past 0.40 are the ROOT POOL** — farther from the centre than any tendril tip, and farther from the depth axis too. | ^ the numbers, above `yFloor`, in units of height | distance from the depth axis p50 0.230 / p90 0.342 / max 0.501 → `dFloor 0.28` + `dSoft 0.14` leaves the median body still, moves 27% partially, 3% fully. The 8 eyes sit at 0.034-0.262, discs reaching 0.295 = weight **0.009** | **landed — `curl 0.13` (~9px at a tip), `rate 0.25`, `lobes 3` (INTEGER, or the azimuth wrap creases the fan)** |
| ^ both gates POSITIONAL, and that is load-bearing | the preservator entry above: a displacement masked by ALBEDO tears a uv seam, because a TRELLIS atlas cuts charts along the high-curvature ridges a colour boundary follows. | **The watcher's eyes are REPAINTED, not animated** | a baked pupil cannot be shifted (shattered atlas, nothing authorable in uv space) and a second drawn over it gives two — so `PropEyes` OVERWRITES `diffuseColor` inside 8 measured discs. `<emissivemap_fragment>` is the right anchor for a diffuse write: it runs BEFORE `lights_physical_fragment`, so the repaint is lit like the flesh round it | **landed — `propAnimCLpropGlowE8`, other three organs byte-identical, 0 new calls/passes/geometry/art** |
| ^ the pupil was driven from POSITION, not from the eyeball's normal | the normal version needs no authored table at all, and was rejected on a mean-normal-magnitude reading that does not actually say what it was read as saying (a ring's normals cancel even on a perfect sphere) | **position shipped and works; the normal route is untested, not disproven** |
| ^ **the repaint left a RING of every baked eye showing** | `step( 0.0, dot( dv, ef ) )` guarded against painting the far side of the body — but the disc's centre sits at the cap's APEX, so the whole eye is at NEGATIVE ax. Measured: the clip rejected **48-76% of every disc**, rising monotonically with radius (inner deciles ~0, rim 8/9) | **replaced by a CYLINDER in the eye's own frame — radial distance perpendicular to its axis, plus an axial slab offset back and tight in FRONT (the cap is 0.16 R deep, the body 4-11 R behind, and a tendril arcing over must not be painted white)** |
| ^ per-triangle CENTROID sampling under-read every radius | a centroid asks "is the MIDDLE of this triangle pale", so every triangle straddling the sclera edge counts as flesh. | ^ **and the check inherited the bug** | the orthographic dump used to eyeball the fit painted ONE CENTROID COLOUR PER TRIANGLE, so it showed the same under-read — and rendered the eyes as flat faceted diamonds, which got written up as a property of the mesh and used to justify the design. | ^ two dots that look like tiny eyes are not eyes | measured as a vestigial bump and a hole through the flesh, no sclera in either | **eight, not ten** |
| **The gaze had to be a SACCADE, not a drift** | "jerkier, like humans look side to side" — a sum of sines sweeps smoothly and reads as floating at ANY amplitude or rate; the shape of the motion was the failure, not its size | **hold + flick, stateless: fixation index is `floor` of the clock, both endpoints hashed off it, so `gk+1` is the next cycle's own start and the handover cannot pop** |
| ^ measured spikiness, not asserted | per-frame change over 5 frames at 0.45s: **mean 14.12 on one interval vs 1.79 / 2.93 / 3.86** on the others. A drift would be flat across all four | **`gazeRate 0.07` (~2.1s hold, 1.6-3.0s across the eyes), `gazeSnap 0.06` (~0.13s flick)** |
| ^ **the FLANK eye cannot be fitted automatically** | one shared outward anchor serves the seven eyes on the prop's face and skews the one round its side, so its radial profile smears and never forms a plateau. Two automated fits disagreed with each other and both with the art; a ring-centroid re-fit converged stably and was right on y but **0.01 short on x AND radius**, 30% at that eye's size | **read off an orthographic dump. A profile that refuses to plateau is the fit telling you it failed — take the number when the shape of the evidence is right, look at the thing when it is not** |
| ^ a per-eye axis was considered and skipped | one number fixed it; a per-eye axis is a field on every row to serve a single case | **`0.266 / 0.599 / r 0.036`** |
| **Tendrils that HINGE are not tendrils: `wave` puts the bend along the limb** | the curl angle depended on a vertex's azimuth and the gate but not on how far OUT it sat, so every vertex of one limb leaned the same and the fan swung as rigid spokes with a taper. A phase term proportional to distance from the axis makes the lean vary down the limb | **`wave 2.5` cycles/height over a 0.22-height moving span = a bit over half an S; SUBTRACTED against the clock so the ripple travels outward. `curl` 0.13 → 0.16, since the middle of an S travels less than an arc's** |
| ^ frame-diffing anything in a TUNNEL is confounded | an A/B of the tendrils read 37.7% of pixels changed at mean 24.43 — `SewerLamps` runs ~30% of fixtures dead and ~35% sputtering, so the lighting moves more between captures than the prop does | **trap — diff where the lamps do not reach, or compare silhouettes against the dark. | ^ magnitude varies and Y is squashed 0.55x | components hashed to -1..1 rather than normalized, so an eye settles near centre as often as hard over. The squash is in WORLD axes, which is correct ONLY because every grown organ is `PropYaw.FRONTAL` — world Y is up on the prop and lies in every eye's tangent plane | **would be wrong on a prop that can be turned** |
| ^ **min/max in sRGB is not min/max in LINEAR** | 0.82 sRGB is ~0.65 linear, so the tight seed threshold found 33 of 123 eye triangles and split the big central eye into five clusters. Greedy clustering is also order-dependent and needs a merge-to-fixed-point pass | **the same class as the albedo-ratio trap above: always ratio in linear** |
| ^ the outward anchor must sit BEHIND the eye field, not among it | all 8 eyes are on one near-flat face at z 0.05-0.12, so their own centroid lands at z 0.10 — in front of the central eye, whose outward vector would then be 0.02 long and point anywhere | **authored `az: -0.35`; Gram-Schmidt, not a `cross(up,fwd)` basis, which blows up on the eyes facing near-straight-up** |
| ^ **the pupils shipped invisible, from three compounding halvings** | scaling the tangential gaze by **1/sqrt(3)** is the true bound on a vec3 of sines and useless — typical length is ~0.8, so the typical throw was 46% of the ceiling; `iris 0.52` left nowhere to go; `gazeRate 0.04` is a ~25s cycle | **CLAMP to unit instead of dividing by the bound; `iris`/`gaze` are ONE decision (their SUM is how near the rim it gets) — 0.42/0.48, rate 0.14. ~1px → ~3px on a 13px eye** |
| ^ a "match the body" colour must be measured where the thing SITS | the blink's `lid` used the prop's whole-body albedo mean (71/41/69) and read as a hole: the root pool and shaded back are half the surface and far darker than the rim an eye sits in. | ^ tendril sweep `rate` 0.25 → **0.125** | the tips travel the same distance either way, so speed only decides whether it reads as drifting or as flicking | **~8s sweep** |
| **The slime trail drew through walls** | its material shipped `depthTest: false` "so the raised curb can't occlude it" — but transparent draws after opaque, so with no depth compare it painted over every wall and prop in front of it. A wall that would occlude the trail is faded by `Occlusion`, and below `ghostCross` the real mesh is hidden while only the `depthWrite:false` ghost draws, so it still reads through exactly like the actor sprites, the blood decals and its own landing puddles** |

| ^ Shift+Space, and `HUD.isVisible()` is the only state | each consumer reads it once per frame; the ring, path preview, tactical grid and badges all re-assert from live state | **no restore path, nothing to desync** |
| **`sink`: an organ that GREW out of the floor should not stand on it** | `normalize` puts every base at y=0 — right for a ladder, wrong for a grown organ whose root rim then reads as a lip laid on top. Worst on the preservator, where the flat root pool is 47% of the surface, so that rim IS the ground-level silhouette | **new `sink` column, a fraction of `h`; biomineral and preservator at 0.05 (0.19 / 0.18 world), arch and watcher stay 0. | **The wilderness becomes a 3D area kind (`render.wild`)** | `AREA_GROUND` was the last common kind on the 2D tile renderer. Third `Area3D`, built from the SAVED cell grid like the tunnels — no seed, every existing save. | ^ the ground is SUBDIVIDED, WELDED and vertex-tinted, per chunk | one quad per cell reads as a tile when there is no kerb, no road paint and no building edge to break the repeat. `SUB` 2 = 80k tris over the area, so one mesh per `Chunks.CELLS` block. Live over the two lab buildings: **538** faces look into rooms, **218** into corridors — 12,912 vs 5,112 units², so corridor wall is **28.4%** of the interior | **one local `look(c,r)` closure replaces the `hangar ‖ !inner` ternary at all three call sites (faces, butt caps, door lintels), +1 merged mesh per non-hangar structure. `wall-interior` stays the CORRIDOR — its prompt already asked for one — and the new art is the ROOM** |
| **A wall cell is the UNION OF ITS RUNS, not their bounding box — Phase 1.7** | five more off walking 1.6: full-cell lumps at every room corner, a seam above and below every window, roof texture still on the outside of the glass, the doorway reveal painted in the wrong room's finish, and the drain grate at twice the size it wants | **landed — 1113 → 1338 shell quads (+450 tris), 0 console errors, no persisted change** |
| ^ **`slab()` is a BOUNDING BOX and four passes read it as the masonry** | where the wall line turns or branches the runs form an L, a T or a cross and the box is strictly bigger: a corner is a 3x3 pier with a 1x1 shoulder standing in the room past both wall lines. Census of the 602 wall+door cells: **535** straight, **22** corners, **44** Ts, **1** crossing — the Ts, at two shoulders each, are the bigger half, and the crossing filled its cell outright | **new `notches()` returns the corner squares (one per PAIR of adjacent carrying directions) and hands each to the DIAGONAL cell, exactly as `strips()` hands a run's leftover to the neighbour beside it: floor, fading ceiling, paint, plus two faces of masonry. **114** squares of 1 unit², **all 114** with an indoor diagonal so `FacilityGround` never sees one. Box 4518 → masonry **4404**** |
| ^ the union is up to three rects, and the "no x-run" early-out is worth a branch | `capTop` emits the x-run's middle band and the z-run's two arms; without the early-out a straight VERTICAL wall emitted its band as three abutting quads | **verified against `box − notches` on all 602 cells, exact to **0**. Roof band 728 → **686** quads even with the corner splits, because the glazed cells stopped emitting an outboard one** |
| ^ butt caps were comparing slab extents, which answers the wrong question | at a corner the box reaches the boundary on the side the OTHER run leaves through, and the difference read as an exposed end — so the cap was holding up a shoulder that should not exist | **compare THICKNESS instead. 385 → **196** butt quads = exactly 49 window runs x 2 ends x 2, i.e. nothing but the glazing reveals the rule was meant to cap** |
| ^ **`rimRect` deleted — the straight roof line was not worth bitumen on the glass** | its only remaining job was running the band out to `WALL_T`'s inset on an OUTDOOR side: a no-op on solid wall, and **126** glazed cells x 2 units² = **252** units² of roof texture sitting on the outside of the panes | **`rimRect ≡ slab` once that goes, so the function goes too and `capTop` picks `head` or `rim` off `glazed` alone. Head cap **504** units² over 126 cells = exactly the 1x4 glass slab; rim/masonry now **1.000** (1.872 before 1.6, 1.056 after)** |
| ^ ~~**`WINDOW_EPS` deleted — the offset WAS the seam**~~ | the pane stood 0.02 proud of the wall "so nothing depends on polygonOffset", but the emitter cuts the opening OUT of the wall rather than painting the pane over it, so the two are never coplanar-and-overlapping | **SUPERSEDED — `WINDOW_OVERLAP` made the premise false and the offset came back. The wall above and below an opening IS on the pane's own plane; while the pane matched the opening they only touched, and the moment it grew past them they overlapped. See the `WINDOW_EPS` row below** |
| ^ the doorway reveal took whichever flanking space the loop read first | so an interior door was painted in the room's tile half the time and an exterior one in the outside cladding | **`st.hangar ? wOut : wCorr` — a doorway belongs to the circulation and not to either space it joins, the same reason the generator floors every door cell in lino. Kills the `soffit == null` bookkeeping** |
| ^ ~~`GRATE_TILE` 4.0 → **2.0**~~ | one tile per cell drew a 2 m square of grating, which reads as a vehicle pit | **SUPERSEDED by the drain row in Phase 1.8 — neither one cell-wide grating nor four of them, because a drain is a fitting IN the floor and an object was already drawing one** |
| **The threshold, the drain and the 2D leftovers — Phase 1.8** | three leftovers of the 2D view showing through the 3D one: a cell-wide drain grating competing with the drain object's own decal, corridor lino laid out past the building line at every exterior door, and the old door tile image lying on every threshold | **landed — 0 console errors, no persisted change** |
| ^ **a drain is a fitting IN the floor, and an object was already drawing one** | the cell carries an `objects.FloorDrain` with `isGroundDecal()` true, so the actor layer had been laying its icon flat all along while `Surf.GRATE` painted a cell-wide grating underneath it. **23** cells | **`Surf.GRATE` falls through `floorBuf` to room floor; the `fGrate` buffer, `FLOOR_GRATE` and `GRATE_TILE` all go. The fix is a deletion** |
| ^ that object's art lives in the ATLAS, so the new grate was pasted into it | `FloorDrain` draws `ROW_OBJECT2`/`FRAME_FLOOR_DRAIN` = cell (16,0) of `app/img/entities64.png`; the old icon is a pale round cover, alpha bbox exactly **32x32 centred in the 64**, mean sRGB **163/170/178** — the brightest thing on a facility floor | **`facility/floor-grate.png` LANCZOS'd to 32x32 into a cleared cell holds that footprint to the pixel (so the decal's size does not move) and lands at **39/45/47**. Traps: `app/` is a gitignored SYMLINK to the packaged runtime, so the atlas is the only copy and has no tracked source; and `make tex` does NOT reach it** |
| ^ **a doorway's floor is the two spaces it joins, not the door cell's own surface** | the door branch capped the SLAB in the door cell's own lino, and a door cell's slab is its LINTEL — masonry above head height with nothing under it — so an exterior door ran 2 units of corridor lino out past the building line | **new `floorStrips()` collapses the slab to the wall LINE and re-runs the same decomposition, which IS the two halves — no second rule. Over 30 doorways / 480 units²: TILE 144 → **288**, LINO 304 → **128**, WALKWAY 24 → **48**, LOT 4 → **8**, CONCRETE 4 → **8**; outdoor threshold **28 → 56**. The CEILING still comes off `strips()`, since the lintel's own top is the rim — the two part company at a doorway and nowhere else** |
| ^ **every facility doorway had its 2D tile image lying on the threshold under the leaves** | `iconOff` is computed as `ObjModels.modelFor(key) != null`, and a swinging leaf pair is not a glb prop, so a door drew both | **`FacilityDoors.draws(area, o)` OR'd into it, scoped to `AREA_FACILITY`: the corporate and underground-lab generators deal `objects.Door` too and nothing draws leaves for those, so there the icon is still all there is. Object marks ride the same flag and doors never had them (`visible()` is false for a door)** |
| **A lab bench is a BOX, so it is merged geometry and not a prop — Phase 2** | the plan wanted two generated glbs (`bench-lab`, `bench-island`); the 2D art draws a lab table as a plain slab with a drawer strip, i.e. a worktop over cabinets — six quads a cell | **neither was generated. 207 bench cells, **2694 tris in 2 draw calls**, arbitrary run lengths with no seam. 0 console errors, no persisted change** |
| ^ **the SHAPE census is what killed the props, not the art** | 207 cells in **81 blocks**: 53 of 2x1, 9 of 3x1, 7 islands of 2x3/3x2 — and **11 blocks that are not rectangles at all**, where a run grew into an island or another run | **a prop needs a mesh per shape and shows a seam at every cell boundary of a run; those 11 have no shape to make one for. Merged geometry never names a shape — a face is emitted toward a neighbour that is not a bench, exactly as the wall pass does, and every generator block falls out of that. `tableOf` is a BLOCK test on tileset rows 8-11 cols 0..7, not a list of the 22 `Const` names** |
| ^ ~~one inset rect covers all four sides~~ | pulling the casework back `BENCH_LIP` 0.2 from every side that does not carry on closes the corners for free, and IS geometrically fine | **SUPERSEDED by the overhang row below — an inset body starts 0.2 past the cell boundary, so its world-aligned `u` starts mid-door and the first and last cabinet of every run get cut** |
| ^ ~~**`BENCH_TILE` wanted to be 2.0, and that is the lattice mistake this file's own note warns about**~~ | so it was set to 1.75, the cabinet's own height, to keep the period OFF the cell grid | **SUPERSEDED — that rule is about IRREGULAR art, and this shipped a visible defect: 4.0/1.75 = 2.29 repeats a cell, the rhythm drifts against the grid, and a run ends on half a drawer** |
| ^ **the overhang belongs to the WORKTOP, not to the casework — a UV decision, not a modelling one** | the fix for both rows above is one idea: grow the top outward instead of pulling the body in, so the body spans exact cell boundaries; and put the period ON the grid at 2.0, because cabinetry IS a regular rhythm and landing it on the grid is the entire point | **every body quad's `u` endpoints land on a WHOLE repeat: `worstOffGrid` **0**, all **1116** endpoints at fraction **0.0**, 2 repeats a face, 4 doors a cell at 0.5 m. A door cannot be cut on any face of any block shape, whatever the art's internal layout. Synthetic single cell: worktop **4.4 x 4.4** = `CELL + 2*LIP`, casework **4.0** exactly** |
| ^ two periods on two axes is why the casework cannot call `FacilityGeom.face` | `u` is world-aligned on `BENCH_TILE`; `v` must run 0..1 over the body height so the bottom-up art lands whole (measured **[0, 1]**). `face` has one `t` | **`FacilityTables.panel`, 8 lines, through the same now-public `FacilityGeom.ends` so the winding stays derived in one place.
