| ^ no soffit under the overhang, no floor under the casework | the ring left open at the top of the body is 0.2 wide | **at 51 degrees a ray entering at the bottom of the 0.25 edge band drops 0.25 units over the 0.2 it travels inward, so it lands on the cabinet face. Nothing to see through. At a CONCAVE corner the grown caps overlap 0.2² — coplanar, same texture, same world UVs, so the same pixel either way — and the edge bands cross by 0.2, which reads as nothing** |
| ^ `bench-body` was the brightest surface in the facility | **0.0891** linear as painted, over `wall-room` 0.0592 and `floor-tile` 0.0607 — a room read as a ring of glowing cabinets | **`lift` 1.23, the same value its wall neighbour needed → **0.0537**. `bench-top` left alone at **0.0203**: a black epoxy worktop being the darkest thing is what gives a bench its silhouette, and the `GROUND_LOT` trap is about a ground PLANE** |
| ^ **`WorldCtx.ground` is what stands a decoration on its bench** | the generator writes the table decoration onto the SAME cell as the table tile, so every flask and microscope sat at y = 0 with a metre of casework in front of it | **`FacilityArea` stops clearing `ground` and points it at `FacilityTables.groundY` — the one hook for this, so sprite, shadow, fade and cursor pick all follow with no code of their own. `floorY(22,27)` and `floorYAt` both **2**, the cell beside it **0**. **175 of 234** decorations are on a bench** |
| ^ **`Decoration.isGroundDecal()` → false, and the planned per-row table was wrong** | the plan had rows 8/13 + 9-11/14 upright and row 12 flat; every icon in rows 8-14 of `entities64` is painted in ELEVATION — fridges, flasks, microscopes, drums, server racks, robot arms — row 12 included, which is barrels and racks and not floor litter | **one override, no table. Laid flat they read as decals of furniture printed on the floor. The only two cells that would genuinely lie down are the gloves. `DecorationExt` keeps the default — it carries a random angle and scale and IS litter** |
| **`Light.layers` cannot light indoors and outdoors differently — DEAD END** | three's docs say a light's layers pick which objects it affects; the renderer tests `object.layers.test( camera.layers )` when it COLLECTS lights (`three.global.js:43178`, `:43351`) | **there is NO per-object light filtering in three. A light is in the whole render or out of it, so two ambient lights cannot be aimed at two sets of meshes. Two render passes are the only layer answer and they cost a second full frame** |
| **Indoor vs outdoor lighting as a world-XZ MASK, not as lights — Phase 3a** | `render.world.IndoorMask`, modelled on `VisionMask`: one blurred step texture sampled after `<lights_fragment_begin>`, swapping `irradiance` and scaling `reflectedLight.direct*` | **landed. No draw call, no pass, one fetch. Movers are free (an actor is lit by where it STANDS, no per-object plumbing) and the transition is SPATIAL (blurred boundary = a doorway crossfade with no eased `t` and nothing to tick)** |
| ^ the mask must be a program PERMUTATION, not a tax | an always-on fetch would cost every lit fragment in every area for a facility-only feature | **gated at the call site: `VisionMask.patch` chains it only while a mask is attached, so a city/tunnel/wilderness surface compiles today's shader. Verified — a wilderness entry added 5 programs, none carrying `indoor`. `Sprites` is the one unconditional caller (its pool fills lazily mid-play, so a per-area decision would mislight a slot or compile an unwarmed program)** |
| ^ **`px: 2` silently halved the boundary — a sub-texel `fillRect` antialiases** | `grow` is a QUARTER of a cell, so at 2 texels per cell the overhang is 0.5px and canvas resolves it as partial coverage, before the blur even runs | **measured: a lab floor cell next to its own wall read **194/255** — 24% outdoors, standing in a room. `px: 8` puts the overhang on a whole texel: outer wall face **1/255**, inner face **240/255**** |
| ^ **the blur is bounded above by the WALL THICKNESS** | a slab is `WALL_T` 2 units through and its two faces must land on opposite ends of the ramp; at `blur 1.4` the inner face read **0.50** and the outer **0.33** — the whole wall lit halfway indoors | **`blur: 0.5`, and `grow: WALL_T * 0.75` — PAST the inner face by that blur, not merely to it. Stopping at `WALL_T / 2` puts the face on the ramp's own midpoint, which IS the 0.50** |
| ^ **`isIndoor` is the wrong predicate: a room divider is not floor** | a 1-cell partition is painted only by `grow` from both sides, leaving its own mass unpainted — 211 cells inside one building read ~0.3 indoor, so dividers stayed moonlit between two lit rooms | **`FacilityModel.isInterior`: floor or wall with a structure owning all four neighbours. 787 → **983** cells at ≥224; **315 of 315** partitions fully indoor on a reroll** |
| ^ **"wall shadows on the pavement behave as if no roof exists" — NOT a shadow, the mask leaking out of every doorway** | an exterior door cell finalises to plain lino, so `isIndoor` paints it whole and `grow` puts interior light on the PAVEMENT and on the outward face of the shut leaves. Reported from the game; the moon toggle barely moved those patches, which is what ruled shadows out | **the same four-neighbour test excludes it (a door has the lot on one side), so the doorway is reached by grow from the room behind exactly as the wall it interrupts is. Isolated by swapping the mask for the black stand-in in a fixed exterior frame: **+11.56** mean RGB / **6.8x** before, **+0.27** / **1.01x** after. All 33 doors of a rerolled facility: interior **255**, exterior **≤76**** |
| ^ a deliberate "free door spill" was the wrong call | the leak was first kept on the reasoning that it previewed 3b's spill cones | **it does not: a spill cone is ADDITIVE and shaped, while this relit the pavement and the shut leaves as though they were indoors. Open-door light belongs to 3b's lighting pass** |
| **A LOAD does not invalidate the 3D view — it keeps the PREVIOUS save's geometry** | reported as walking through every wall into the hangar, and as being blocked with no wall in sight. `GameScene.draw3D` and `View.showFacility` both skip a rebuild on the AREA ID, ids are persisted, and a load hands the same ids to new `AreaGame`s. An area generates on FIRST VISIT, so loading an older save while standing in a lab regenerates it under the same id | **`View.forget()` + `GameScene.forget3D()` from `Game.applyLoaded` — BOTH guards or the other still skips. Shown model vs live grid: **0 of 4500** cells before, **4500 of 4500** after. Reproduced and re-verified by loading twice with the view live** |
| ^ the mirror that was NOT a transform bug | the shown model matched the live grid on 100% of cells **under a row mirror**, which reads exactly like a flipped axis | **it is not: the generator picks which edge takes the entrance road, so two rolls of one area are exact mirrors. `shownKey` matching the current area id is what identified it as a stale scene, and a forced rebuild gave **100.0% direct**. Check the identity before believing a transform** |
| ^ `indoorDirect` scales EVERY direct light, not just the moon | `<lights_fragment_begin>` sums point, spot and directional into one `reflectedLight` and there is no anchor between them | **accepted and compensable: a fixture hung INSIDE is scaled by exactly this number, so phase 3b's ceiling lights are tuned WITH it in view. An outdoor lamp is untouched (its fragments read `inAmt` 0). `direct: 0.3`** |
| ^ warm parity | the mask adds a second `customProgramCacheKey`, the same thing that forced a separate facility warm scene at 91 → 101 programs | **`View.warmup` attaches around the facility block only, and re-attaches around the async park-plant callback. Second facility entry adds **0** programs. 0 console errors** |
| **"building shadows STILL have window holes" — the opening is a REAL hole in the only mesh that casts** | over a window run the wall emits faces only below the sill and above the head, and of the shell's wall buffers only `wOut` casts while `addPane` set `castShadow = false`. So the moon went through the glass and put one clean parallelogram on the floor inside the wall's own shadow, per run. NOT the roof — `addRoof` not casting is deliberate (the moon box follows the player) | **`mesh.castShadow = true` in `addPane`. Isolated by flipping it on the live meshes with the camera pinned: **49,303** changed px in four parallelograms, max delta **406/765**, nothing else moving. Cost **70 → 72** calls, **+1** program, 196 tris** |
| ^ why one line is enough and not a first approximation | a half-transparent `alphaTest` pane and two coplanar faces per run both look like they need special handling | **the depth material carries `alphaTest` 0.02, so the glass casts SOLID — right for a night exterior, where a window reads as part of the wall. And `shadowSide` flips `FrontSide` to `BackSide`, so the pass takes exactly the face looking AWAY from the moon: no acne, no double-cast. A shadow-only quad or a second uncut wall buffer buys nothing and costs a buffer** |
| **A VENT is cut into a wall, and was drawn lying on the floor inside it** | reported as a dotted through-wall mark on the ground that "needs to be on the wall". The generator writes `TEMP_BUILDING_VENT` as `TILE_BUILDING` and stands an `objects.Vent` on it; `isGroundDecal` defaults true, so the icon lay flat two units inside a `WALL_T` slab and the only thing ever visible was `paintObjMark`'s `GreaterDepth` silhouette — the bug reported itself, in dots. The 5 vents are the ONLY objects on a wall cell (247 objects), always in a straight run | **a third `WorldCtx` area-kind hook beside `ground`/`climbArc`: `wallMounts(col,row)` → one pose per EXPOSED face, **null** elsewhere so the actor layer allocates nothing in the common case. `Sprites.paint` grew a third pose (flat / **wall face** / frontal), `faceRotY` = the `paintWall` pose, forwarded by `paintObjMark` too. `MOUNT_Y 4.0`, `MOUNT_SCALE 0.5`, `MOUNT_PROUD 0.12`** |
| ^ **painting BOTH faces stamped a permanent dotted GHOST on the roof** | the far face holds an identical quad its own masonry hides, and a hidden quad is exactly what the through-wall mark draws — so the near face showed the grille while the far face's mark hung 60 px above it | **only the side whose normal faces the camera: `(camPos − pose) · (sin rotY, cos rotY) > 0`. Measured at the reported cell — south **+18.88**, north **−21.12**, one quad drawn. The face's yaw is `atan2(DC[dir], DR[dir])` — the normal IS the direction vector, so no rotation table and no crossing `Geom`'s different dir numbering** |
| ^ the WorldCtx hooks are cleared at BUILD time, and `View` skips rebuilds | reading `WorldCtx` in the same tick as a `teleport` showed the facility's hook still live in a city | **not a leak: `GameScene.draw3D` rebuilds on the NEXT frame. One frame later it reads null in a city and in the wilderness, with `ground`/`climbArc` still set where they belong. Cost one false positive — check a frame later before believing a stale hook** |
| ^ **the new `facility/vent` art came back at the WALL's own value and vanished** | the old flat icon was 108/121/121 sRGB against `wall-exterior`'s 44/50/57 — more than twice the wall, which is the only reason it read. The generated grille is honestly the same steel, 51/60/66, and measured **6.9 luma against the wall's 7.6** in the built frame: **0.9x** | **`lift` 0.7 → 81/92/98, frame **14.6 vs 7.6 = 1.93x**. Deliberately UNDER the old 3.1x: the old plate had no internal contrast so brightness was all it had, this one carries its own slats. Net prominence still up — 2x linear is 4x area, 1568 px at 1.93x vs 464 px at 3.1x** |
| ^ the atlas paste keeps the OLD icon's bbox, and the 3D size is a separate knob | the icon lives in `entities64.png` and is drawn by the 2D view too, so growing the art would have grown it there | **pasted at **38x40 on (13,12)** — the old icon's alpha bbox, which is exactly 1520 opaque px = 38x40, so it was a solid rect and the footprint transfers to the pixel. `MOUNT_SCALE` 0.5 → 1.0 and `MOUNT_Y` 4.0 → 2.0 do the in-game size and height alone. `make tex` does NOT reach the atlas — LANCZOS by hand into a cleared cell, same trap as `facility/floor-grate`** |

### FX, UI and the view itself

| change | result | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Slime trail (crawl ribbon + landing puddle) | hand-built strip, **1 draw call**, ~26 verts | **landed** |
| Mouse move-path preview + click-to-move in 3D | **2 draw calls** while a walkable tile is hovered | **landed** |
| Rename `StreetView` → `render.View`, `#streetview` → `#view` | pure rename, no render change | **landed** |
| `vidBrightness` (50–150%, default 100) | exposure is a per-frame uniform: no recompile, no re-assert | **landed** |
| Lobbed projectiles (`arc`) + a `'blood'` projectile type | 3 extra sprites per swing on the existing pooled batch | **landed** |
| **Facility Phase 3b: the compound gets light SOURCES — masts, ceiling fixtures, window spill** | 3a made the interior its own lighting ENVIRONMENT and gave it no sources: rooms lifted uniformly (which never says WHERE the light is), the lot lit by nothing but the moon, and a lit building giving away nothing from across it. Placement is off the tile grid, deterministic, no rng: a three-row table of (surface stood on, surface bordered) — walkway/road, lot/walkway, grass/walkway — gated on `(col + row) % 6`, plus one fixture per `m.rooms` entry | **35 masts, 25 fixtures over 25 rooms, 12 pool slots claimed. **0 new programs** — `FacilityScene` has built the full 12-slot pool with no bulbs since phase 0 for `NUM_SPOT_LIGHTS` parity, so the lights were already in every program key and already running their loop. `LampShadows` and the 512² caster maps arrive free off `active()`. Nothing flickers, so `LightCone.pulse` is never called and the per-frame cost is one `update()`** |
| ^ **an additive quad authored against a 0..1 intuition ANNIHILATES this art** | `C_POOL` derived from `LAMP_COLOR`'s `Y_linear` 0.435 gave 0.25, which measured **Y 0.530 against a lit lab floor's 0.0168 — 31x the surface it lies on**. The error is in the SURFACES, not the blend: floor albedo is 0.0607 linear and interiors render around **Y 0.017**, so nothing here is predictable from the additive colour | **fit off the built frame instead: ~**1.3 per unit C**, not the 2.05 one blown-out sample suggested — so the first correction (0.006, 1.47x) undershot as badly as 0.25 overshot. Landed `C_POOL` **0.012 = 1.93x** the same room's plain floor. Spill 0.02 puts the pavement under an opening at 0.020-0.038 against 0.006-0.009 in the gaps, an 18-sample scan** |
| ^ ONE merged additive mesh for four kinds of quad | lamp heads, ceiling panels, room floor pools and window/door spill differ only in BRIGHTNESS | **which rides `MeshBufTools.quadC`'s per-vertex grey, so one material carries hue + HDR multiplier and one draw call carries the lot. `vertexColors` IS a program key — warmed in `facScene`. The ceiling panel is its own mesh, NOT in `Shell.roof`, so it survives the fade; from outside the opaque roof depth-rejects it, because additive with `depthWrite` off still depth-TESTS** |
| ^ the prop's head is NOT at its placement point | `Models.normalize` centres the bbox in X/Z, and this glb's mast sits at local **z −0.062** with the housing hanging off toward +z — a bulb at the placement point hangs in mid air beside the fixture | **`LAMP_HEAD_Y/Z` measured off the built glb, and `LAMP_H` 5.9 is chosen so `LAMP_H * LAMP_HEAD_Y` = **5.60** = the city street bulb's own height, landing `LampShadows`' `refY / post.y` on exactly 1.0. `DoorCell.inDir` is the side the LEAVES SWING into, not the side the lot is on — the spill finds the outdoor side itself, on the perpendicular axis only** |
| ^ **the facility warm scene never renders, so its `depth` programs were never warmed** | a facility entry adds 5 programs, all `depth` — and adds the SAME 5 with the whole lighting pass switched off behind a temporary flag (masts 0, ceilings 0) | **so 3b costs nothing, and the 5 are a STANDING GAP: `compileAsync` does not compile shadow-map depth programs, and only the CITY warm scene is ever rendered (`comp.render()` at the end of warmup). A city entry shows the same shape at 12 (5 depth + 5 lambert + 2 physical). Not fixed here** |
| ^ **the head quad was the wrong tool, and a TRELLIS atlas does NOT have to be hand-painted** | a FRONTAL quad is fixed-yaw by construction; a mast YAWS to face the surface it lights, so the card sat across the housing at an arbitrary angle and read as a sticker. `3d-models.md`'s "hand-painting an emissive map into an unknown atlas layout is not worth it" is true and does not apply — it can be GENERATED | **rasterize the UV triangles whose centroid is above y 0.44 and past z 0.035 in the model's own frame (the head box, not the arm) into a 512 mask, dilate 2px into the chart gutter, tint with `LAMP_COLOR` because `models.mjs` pins `emissiveFactor` to `[1,1,1]`, 0 new programs. Rasterize off the BUILT glb, never the 100k master, or the mask misses a chart the decimation moved** |
| ^ **the whole head box was too much, and the FIX for it was the wrong theory** | the head is a flat SLAB, so its biggest face is the top, pointing straight at a camera that looks down: it read as a glowing white card. Filtering by averaged `NORMAL` (`ny < -0.5`) was then reported as still wrong, with white polys on the pole, blamed on a generated mesh's normals | **the normals were fine. Measured instead of believed: rasterize the mask, test every UNSELECTED triangle's UV centroid against it — **22 sampled a lit texel, y −0.498..+0.484, z −0.057**, i.e. the mast down to the base plate. It was the **2px DILATION**, which on a mosaic atlas reaches the next chart, and that chart is an unrelated part of the surface** |
| ^ **[`tools/emissive.py`](../tools/emissive.py): a box, a bleed mask, and a preview you can look at** | selection is a bounding box on the triangle centroid, no normals; the dilation is masked against the coverage of every unselected triangle, so it may enter the gutter and never another chart | **the bleed count is the gate. Author at the entry's own `tex` (320), never 512: `models.mjs` resizes it and the resize re-introduces the ramp the mask just removed. The 3-view preview is framed ON THE SELECTION, since a lens is 2% of a 6-metre mast** |

Invisible to the bleed mask, still sampled by the GPU. The bleed CHECK had the matching hole: it tested each unselected triangle's UV CENTROID, and a triangle with one lit corner still draws a speck. Re-audited, the "clean" mask bled onto **9 triangles, y −0.476..+0.463, z −0.074** — the pole | **rasterize CONSERVATIVELY: 4x supersample max-pooled down (a texel is covered if ANY part of a triangle falls in it) + `outline=255` for a triangle even that misses, and grow the unselected coverage 1 texel before subtracting, because bilinear reads a 2x2 neighbourhood. The fixtures themselves still exist and still claim spotlight slots — only the two drawn quads are gone, and the merged glow mesh survives on window/door spill alone at the same draw call** |
| ^ **a rectangle of light reads as a projected slide, not as an aperture** | the spill patch was a rect, and light out of a finite opening fans — every point of the aperture lights every point of the ground | **`SPILL_SPREAD` 4.0 per side over `SPILL_DEPTH` 8.0 = a ~27 degree fan. The uv stays 0..1 so the shared gradient still fits the trapezoid: two numbers, no extra geometry, no extra draw. Wide enough to read, narrow enough that neighbouring runs do not merge into one band** |
| ^ ^ **and the patch STILL read round, because the alpha map is a squircle** | `makeRectGlowGradient`'s corner radius is 0.16 of the canvas against a half-extent of 0.30 — **0.14 of straight run**, so the shape is nearly a circle. Right for the sewer wall fixture's diffuser it was written for, wrong for ground cut by the straight jambs of an opening; worst on a DOOR, whose patch is one cell wide and read as a blob | **optional `radFrac`, default 0.16 (sewer byte for byte), 0.0 for the facility. Measured on the generated canvases: corner texel (14,14) **233 → 255**, diagonal (8,8) **0 → 92**, the straight edges and the row profile identical. The falloff still curves round the corner, which is what a soft edge does** |
| ^ **a window casts NO fake actor shadow, because every caster came from `LampLights.active()`** | `View` hands that one array to `Actors.setLamps`, so a light with no pool slot is invisible to `LampShadows` — and a window must not take a slot, since 12 are already shared by the masts and the ceiling fixtures | **new `Area3D.shadowLamps()`: a static built-once `LampPost` list that reaches `LampShadows` and nothing else, `[]` in the city/tunnels/wilderness where every light does own a slot. One source per CELL of a run, not per run — reach is 3 cells and a 3-cell run is 3 cells long, so a centred source would shadow ALONG the wall** |

Only a 2x2+ blob drops an interior corner, and one missing cross reads as nothing | **second pass in the same `build()`: dashed perimeter (edges facing a WALKABLE neighbour only) + diagonal hatch. | ^ the hatch is a WORLD-space line family clipped per cell, and `HATCH` 2.0 was wrong | `x + z = k * HATCH`, so a stripe continues into the next blocked cell instead of stopping at the shared edge and a thicket hatches as ONE shape. `'diag'` was the last unclaimed fill in the game's pattern vocabulary (XRAY took `scan`, OBJMARK took `dots`) | **at 2.0 (3 stripes/cell) three long strokes read as random SCRATCHES, and the corner-to-corner stripe at 5.66 units beat the 0.6-unit dashes it sits inside. 1.0 (7 stripes) reads as fill. Every reason the guard gives is absent in a facility: the wall is a half-cell slab with real floor either side (`FacilityGeom.strips`), `floorY` is 0, no `Occlusion` runs there. Measured in one window: **330** blocked, **186 skipped**, 144 drawn (38 of them windows)** |

| ^ **the park is a layout now, and its path is plain WALKWAY on purpose** | the old pass was a flat per-cell 10/30/60 roll — even scrub with no shape, 42% of the lawn unwalkable | **a 2-cell path down the long axis arrives KERBED AND LIT for free, because the ground pass and `FacilityLamps`' lit-line table both key on a walkway boundary. Costs no tile, no migration, and dresses a park saved yesterday** |
| ^ **the SPLIT RATIO lied about the route and the COMPONENTS gate was right** | the 100k master measured **1.30x**, inside the "offline decimate works" band, so it took the master-plus-meshopt route — and meshopt floored at **9,277 tris**, twice the budget for a cylinder, with the BUILT glb at **3.86x and 27 components at 43.6%**. On screen: torn polygons, smeared texture | **the ratio describes the MASTER's atlas and says nothing about what the decimation does to it — measure components on the BUILT glb, and do not talk a 27-at-43.6% reject into "body + lid + base ring + liner". Generated AT the budget instead (`decimation_target` 5000, the API floor — 3k is not reachable): **4,765 tris**, `tris: -1`, **2 components at 58.2%** (body + lid, the shipped conifer's 51/49 shape). Runware unwraps and bakes AFTER its own decimation, so the atlas is authored FOR 4,765 tris and `tex 512` lands on **55** with no sizing arithmetic. `dropMR` — and its MR is a per-RUN property: this run read metal max 178, the master run off the SAME reference read **1**** |
| ^ **a prop's own FRONT is measured, and a hole is measured by the surface it REMOVES** | the bin's mouth is local **+z** where the bench's front is local **+x**, so handing it the bench's yaw put the opening 90° off, looking along the seat | **bin the lid band's area into 12 azimuth sectors: the two straddling +z come back at **0.054 / 0.055 against the other ten's 0.085**. Two things did NOT answer it — the lid's centroid (0.0017 off a 0.27 radius; the cap is symmetric either side of the cut) and the darkest 20% of its texture (found the shadowed side, coherence 0.36). A flat-shaded orthographic TURNTABLE showed it, and per-triangle colour must be sampled at SEVEN barycentric points and medianed: one UV-centroid sample lands in the gutter often enough to speckle a TRELLIS prop grey and read as a texture bug that is not there. `bins` holds `look` for the placement frame and turns the instance `look + PI/2` — mouth · path = **1.0000** over all four directions** |
| **The boot warm gets a SCREEN, because it was never invisible** | it fired 300ms AFTER the menu painted, and its head is one synchronous block (3 `CityGen` + 3 `World.build` + a sewer + a facility), so the menu appeared, started its 30fps `MainMenuGL` loop and froze | **`#warmup` in `app.html` — black, `warmup phase` + dots — up from the document's FIRST paint (static markup, before `parasite.js` parses) and removed by `Main` only when the warm settles; `UISTATE_MAINMENU` moved into that reveal. `warmup` grew `?onDone` and a `catchError` it never had — with the menu gated on it, a chain that never settles is a black screen the player cannot leave** |
| ^ **the warm was 7.6s and 90% of it was ART, not shaders** | new timings on the existing log line: **sync 636ms / total 7593ms**, repeatable, 104 programs. the 6.9s is 20 glb reads + the ~20 blob texture decodes GLTFLoader makes from their embedded atlases | **so the warm SPLIT by area kind: boot warms the city (`warmup`), and the tunnels and the facility each warm on the first entry to that kind (`warmKind`, called from `GameScene.draw3D` inside the `fader.cover` callback, under black already up). Boot **7593 → 4625ms**, 104 → **72** programs; facility **+11 in 1695ms**, tunnels **+20 in 345ms**, a repeat call **0ms**. 72+11+20 = 103 vs 104 — one short, not chased. | ^ **the real test is not "IS IT A BOX" — it is whether the object is carried by its SILHOUETTE or by its SURFACE** | "a box is not a prop" is a rule about shape, and it let an arm-plus-cabinet through on the grounds that two boxes at right angles are a shape worth generating. But a boom barrier is not carried by its outline at all — **it is carried by its hazard bands**, and the bake returned orange chevrons on tan where the reference painted red on off-white | **a silhouette is what a mesh generator is for; a stripe pattern is what a TILING TEXTURE is for. Side ABOVE front by the rack's own margin: a front that is half black louvre slot measures darker than the plain flank it shares paint with. Chroma 9.1 cool cast kept — the frames it replaces run 0.6 to 32.4 and the blue ones are the read the prompt asked for** |
| ^ **the row-12 placement trap was real, and the fallback looked wrong on screen** | `nextToWindow` FORCES the low list beside glazing and `isSolidWall` rejects `Wall.WINDOW`, so `FacilityDeco.wall()` returned -1 and the stood-square-in-the-cell fallback fired — a machine floating a clear half cell off the glass with floor all round it | **`wall()` takes masonry on any hand FIRST, then glazing, for a LOW kind only — the rule `placeRound` already ran the drums on. The halo does NOT follow: `hasWall` became an `isSolidWall` test of the CHOSEN hand, or a wall shadow lands on the pane. **The sill is 0.93**, not the worktop height the plan assumed (a 4.14-tall pane centred in `WALL_H` 6.0), so a 2.2 machine takes the bottom third of the opening — which is where one stands anyway** |
| **A WINDOW LOOKED THE SAME FROM BOTH SIDES — and splitting the buffer was the WRONG fix** | reported standing in a lab: every pane a white glowing slab inside as out, same bloom halo, and the lot behind the glass WASHED BRIGHTER instead of darkened. `FacilityGeom.structure` pushes a run's exterior and interior quads into the same `MeshBuf`, picked by RUN LENGTH alone, so one material serves both facings. Live: **4 pane meshes**, 30+14 = 44 quads on the 3-cell art (**22 runs x 2 faces**), 18+24 = 42 on the 2-cell (**21 x 2**), 22+21 = **43** = `__facility().windows`, all at `emissiveIntensity` 2.2 | **one per-structure material lerp, NOT a second buffer: `Shell.panes` carries the 0..2 materials, `FacilityArea.glaze` drives `emissiveIntensity` **2.2 → 0** and `color` **ffffff → 0x39424a** on the roof's own `REVEAL_MULT` clock. **+0 meshes, +0 materials by construction** — nothing is created, so there is no draw call or program to A/B. No geometry, no art, no persisted change** |
| ^ **the SECOND half of the ask is what rules the split out** — the outer face must also stop blooming indoors | the camera pitches 51° down from OUTSIDE the building, so the face it reads at the NEAR wall of the room the player stands in is the EXTERIOR one; and the interior face cannot be seen at all while a structure is solid, its own roof being over it. Both faces want the same value in both states | **the split buys nothing and costs 4 → 8 pane meshes, a program, and the caster below. "Dark from inside" and "no bloom on the outer part indoors" are ONE switch, not two features** |
| ^ **THE SHADOW CASTER IS THE INTERIOR QUAD**, which is exactly what the split would have broken | `side` is `FrontSide`, so three forces `shadowSide = BackSide` and the depth pass draws the face pointing AWAY from the moon — the inward one | **an interior-only mesh with `castShadow` off re-opens the "window holes in building shadows" row above, which `castShadow = true` on the pane was added to close** |
| ^ **keyed off the player's OWN CELL (`model.owner`), NOT off `reveal`** | `reveal` also opens a structure the player is merely PEEKING into from the lot (`PEEK_CELLS` 3), and dimming a facade you are standing outside and looking at is the one thing this must not do | **same easing loop, second target array, no second clock. Verified live: the structure stood in reads **0 / 39424a** on both meshes, the other stays **2.2 / ffffff**, `FacilityEntry`'s opaque booth band untouched at 1.4, and `transparent` / `alphaTest` 0.02 / `castShadow` unchanged throughout** |
| ^ "disable the bloom" needed no pass change, because bloom here is arithmetic on the pane | ONE global `UnrealBloomPass` (threshold **0.9**, strength 0.25), no layers, no selective mask | **glass band linear 0.26-0.62, so **0.9 / 0.62 = 1.45** is where the brightest texel stops crossing — dark enough for the bloom pass, nowhere near dark enough for the eye. Hence 0 and not 1.4. Darkening the lot THROUGH the opening is then free: the glass half is alpha 128, so the pane composites `0.5 * tint + 0.5 * outside`** |

## Standing notes

- **three is vendored** as `electron/three.global.js`, built from `tools/three-entry.js` via
  `node tools/build-three.mjs` (**no `make three` target** despite the comment). Core objects
  (`BatchedMesh`) are already bundled via `export * from 'three'`; `examples/jsm` ones
  (`GTAOPass`, `mergeGeometries`) need an explicit export **and** an entry in the `REQUIRED` guard.
- **`Occlusion` is the constraint on every batching idea.** Buildings must fade independently, so any
  merge must stay inside one building, or ship an ownership map. `pick()` size-guards city-wide
  meshes out (they land in `__occ.skipped()`), and a `userData.b` tag overrides the guard.
- **Batch only geometry that survives culling.** `BatchedMesh` frustum-tests every instance on the CPU
  each frame, so batching mostly-culled objects is a pessimization; an off-screen `InstancedMesh` is
  one bounding-sphere reject.
- **Per-call cost is not uniform.** Shadow draws are depth-only and averaged ~9.5µs against ~23µs for
  the survivors. Never convert a call count into a `submit` estimate.
- **An object that renders correctly only because of a material flag is a bug waiting for the next
  override pass.** `scene.overrideMaterial` replaces the material outright and drops `colorWrite`,
  `depthTest` and `polygonOffset`. Prefer real geometric separation, or `allowOverride: false`.
- **If an overlay is coplanar with a wall, give it a physical gap** (`OVERLAY_EPS`). `polygonOffset`
  alone has failed three times.
- **Anything that mutates an instance buffer after build must tell the `Occlusion` ghost** — the ghost
  is a snapshot taken at first fade, and it is the copy that draws while the real mesh is hidden.
- **A "turn it off" effect has to turn off every source.** A lamp has four: the pooled `SpotLight`, the
  additive `LightCone`, the fake ground shadow, and the glb's **emissive head** (independent of every
  light in the scene, and it feeds bloom directly).
- **`receiveShadow` is a uniform; `castShadow` is baked into the program key.** Flipping the first is
  free per-frame. Do not generalise from one to the other.
- `addRoofDetails` uses `Math.random()` for the type shuffle and yaw, so roof furniture is **not**
  seed-deterministic (unlike `Debris`) and reshuffles on re-entry. Unexamined; may be intentional.
- **ANYTHING THAT SITS ON A SURFACE WANTS A HALO UNDER IT, AND THAT IS THE DEFAULT — not a case to be
  argued each time.** A new prop, fitting, box or piece of kit gets `FacilityDetail.pool` on the
  surface it stands on, and `FacilityDetail.stand` on the wall behind it **when it is near one and is
  a large cuboid** — that second half is the qualifier, not the first. The whole family is a
  collector, one merged mesh and one material, so **the Nth halo is +2 triangles and nothing else**;
  the cost of a new emitter is one `stand`/`pool` call beside the `ContactMask.prop` the builder is
  already making. The interior fill is normal-blind by construction (`IndoorMask` is
  AmbientLight-shaped), so nothing else in the compound will ever ground an object for you — an
  ungrounded thing indoors is not a lighting bug that will get fixed later, it is a missing quad.
- **A HALO SAYS "THIS IS TOUCHING THAT", so the thing that decides whether one is emitted is the
  DISTANCE, and the distance is measured off the SLAB FACE and never off the cell.**
  `FacilityGeom.slab` insets a solid wall's room face a whole unit into the wall cell (a glazed one
  1.5), so anything built on its own cell — a bench — stands that far clear of the masonry and gets
  **no halo at all**: at 0.8 the quad reads as a stain on the wall, and moving the geometry to earn
  one was built and reverted. Anything built off the slab FACE — the fittings, the corridor casework,
  the lab kit — does touch, and takes one. If a new family stands against a wall, keep its back at
  **`FacilityStyle.STAND_GAP`**, which exists only to clear `HALO_EPS`: closer than that and the halo
  plane is INSIDE the object, cutting its side faces and drawing over them.

## Open leads (not yet acted on)

### Walls cost 3 calls per box — an atlas will NOT fix it, a texture array would

`flattenBox`'s floor is **one call per distinct texture**, so a building box costs 3 (walls, worn,
roof) and a parapet 2. In one follow-view census that was ~10 boxes × 3 = 30 calls; collapsing them
to 1 each saves ~20 of 158. **Unmeasured beyond that sample — get a real A/B before believing it.**

**Why the decal fix does not transfer.** Walls *tile*: `repeat.set(wWorld / TILE, …)`
(`Buildings.hx:72`) runs UVs 0→4 across a face and the wrap mode turns `u = 3.2` back into `0.2`,
which is what gives real brick at any building size without stretching. Wrapping is a property of the
**whole texture**, not of a sub-rectangle inside it — walk `u` past your cell's edge in an atlas and
you do not restart the brick, **you slide into the neighbouring image**. Decals could be atlassed
precisely because a decal quad samples its cell **once** (UV 0→1, nothing to wrap). Same word,
different problem. `fract()` in the shader fakes the wrap but breaks mipmapping — the UV jump at each
tile seam blows up the derivatives and the GPU picks the blurriest mip, a visible seam per tile
(fixable with `textureGrad` + padding, i.e. owning custom sampling for every wall in the game).

**The right tool is `DataArrayTexture`**: N full textures as layers, sampled
`texture(tMap, vec3(u, v, layer))`. Each layer is its own complete 0→1 space, so wrapping, mipmaps and
anisotropy all work per layer and brick in layer 3 cannot reach layer 4. `flattenBox` already bakes
per-face texture matrices into the geometry UVs — this adds a per-face `layer` attribute beside them
plus an `onBeforeCompile` patch to sample the array (same move as `instanceUV` in `DecalBatch`).

**The strategic half is bigger than the ~20 calls.** Every wall box would share **one material**,
which is the precondition for welding buildings into a few giant meshes — see the two-tier merge lead
below. The array does not remove that plan's real blocker (`Occlusion` fades one building at a time),
it removes the texture obstacle under it.

**Cost:** all layers must share size + format; the array is built by blitting every wall PNG into one
buffer (build step or load-time canvas); ~20 layers × 512² ≈ 20MB + mips; per-texture `roughness` has
to go uniform or ride another attribute; and every wall material becomes a custom shader we own.
Subsystem-scale. Weigh against a frame that currently runs **158 calls / 4.66ms submit / 11.4ms idle**
— i.e. ~30% used. **Measure on the weak-hardware target before spending this.**

### Two-tier static city merge — for the debug free-cam, not for players

**Read the scope line first:** this targets the **street-debug free-cam at a parallel angle**, the one
view that renders the whole city. The goal was making that dev view usable, not player framerate.

Gameplay does not need it, and that is measured, not assumed: the city is **226–233 buildings**
(`CityGen.generate`, seeds 1-3), while gameplay runs **158–288 calls** (follow 158, zoom-out ~202,
tactical 288). Fog + the far-plane clip + frustum culling already discard ~200 of ~230 buildings
before a draw is issued. **Do not cite this entry as a player-facing perf win.**

The idea: the city is static, so weld every piece sharing a texture into a few giant meshes — "all
brick walls citywide", "all coping", "all roofs" — ~10–20 meshes for the whole map. The free-cam then
draws ~200 calls instead of ~3,000. Standard merged-static-geometry technique.

**The blocker is `Occlusion`.** It fades an *individual* building while it blocks the camera→player
sightline, by owning that building's materials and easing their opacity. One welded mesh + one
material = all-or-nothing fading. The fix is tiering:

- **Near tier** — the handful of buildings that can actually occlude the player. Stays on today's
  individual fadeable meshes, code path unchanged.
- **Far tier** — everything else, welded, never fades (too far to occlude anyway).
- Re-tier on **area movement**, not per frame, re-welding when a building crosses the boundary.
  `Occlusion` only ever sees the near tier.

Notes: welding reuses the bake already written for `flattenBox`/`mergeBand` (per-face UV transform →
geometry UVs, world transform → verts) — the merge is the same vertex concat, and the new work is the
tiering + re-weld bookkeeping. Weld granularity is capped at one mesh per texture because each wall
texture is its own `Texture`; a `DataArrayTexture` (lead above) would lift that cap, though it does
**not** touch the `Occlusion` blocker. Transparent/cutout passes (windows are already `InstancedMesh`,
doors are alphaTest) can stay as-is. Watch the near/far boundary for pop — geometry is identical
either side (same bake), only fadeability changes.

**Verdict: deferred.** Only path to a big cut in the free-cam view, but it is a real subsystem —
tiering, `Occlusion` rework, re-weld on movement. Player-facing perf does not need it. Do it only if
the debug free-cam's usability is worth a subsystem.

### The floor — 32% of the frame, never attacked

Raw texture bandwidth, overdraw, the two RGBA16F composer targets and the `OutputPass` blit, measured
with all lighting and post off. Invariant across lamp counts (4.69 at pool 12, 4.55 at pool 0), which
is what makes it a real target rather than a measurement artefact. Suspects: stacked transparent
overlays (grime, wall decals, storefront bands and doors all draw *over* the wall via `polygonOffset`,
each a full lit fragment pass), and the FP16 composer target format. **No existing debug key isolates
overlay overdraw** — that instrumentation is the first step.
